# 401: Hatching! Brave? No, Just a Glutton!

"It's hatching now?" Cynthia looked utterly shocked.

Hatching amid explosions…

What kind of birth ceremony was this?

"On the day of your birth, the entire Dandelion Island erupted in blasts, cheering for your arrival!"

Gengar and the others also noticed the changes in the Pokémon Egg, turning their gazes toward it in unison.

Meanwhile, the Golem continued their self-destruct spree.

"Ku!!"

Kubfu's expression turned serious.

We must welcome our new companion with a glorious victory!

It was said that the bigger the commotion at birth, the greater the accomplishments in the future!

Hearing this, the little ones all rallied.

"Goka!"

Charcadet carefully held onto the now-cracked Pokémon Egg.

If it weren't for the fact that it had to keep holding the egg, it would have already charged at those Golem to knock them all out!

"Pokémon eggs need a quiet environment to hatch!"

Cynthia's voice was firm as she waved her hand. "Garchomp, Lucario—end the battle quickly!"

Johan's eye twitched.

A… quiet… environment?

"Dragon Rush!"

"Garrr—!!"

"Draaa—!!"

Garchomp and Dragonite both cloaked themselves in a massive aura of blue and purple dragon energy, then dove straight down from the sky.

BOOM! BOOM!!

Thunderous explosions erupted.

Crack! Crack!

The Pokémon Egg's surface was now covered in deep fissures.

It was like a bizarre symphony—one side exploding, the other hatching.

Tinkaton swung its hammer, Kubfu threw punches, Ninetales froze enemies in place, Gengar launched Shadow Balls…

Under their relentless attacks, the swarm of Golem was finally taken down, and the rockslide disaster was gradually put to an end.

With their foes defeated, the Pokémon all rushed back, gathering around the egg at a slight distance.

"Cha…"

Tinkaton blinked, absentmindedly stroking the handle of its hammer.

Am I about to become an older sister?

Even though it was the third Pokémon to join Johan's team, in terms of age, it was easily the youngest of the bunch.

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar rested its chin on its hand, looking deeply thoughtful.

How should 'Johan's Hypnosis Technique' be passed down this time?

As the first inheritor of "Johan's Hypnosis," Gengar was seriously struggling with this dilemma.

Johan stepped forward, taking a deep breath.

No extravagant expectations—

As long as it hatched safely, that was enough!

He didn't care if it was naughty, gluttonous, or even if it couldn't eat—he just wanted the hatching process to go smoothly.

Cynthia stood beside him, feeling a rare sense of emotion.

It was such a privilege to witness the birth of a Pokémon again.

This was the beginning of a new life. A continuation of bonds.

When life met life, something new was always born.

At that moment, Cynthia found herself wanting to raise another Pokémon from an egg…

"It's happening," Cynthia whispered.

Johan nodded, keeping his eyes locked on the Pokémon Egg.

Crack! Crack—!

As the shell fractured, a small, dull-green horn poked through, pressing against the egg's surface.

Then, two tiny hands wiggled out, waving adorably.

The hatching was progressing smoothly.

"Looks like… a Larvitar?" Cynthia whispered.

"Haha…"

Johan let out a pained chuckle.

Even though he had mentally prepared himself, his heart still sank just a little.

But—

He quickly adjusted his mindset.

Healthy and happy—that was all that mattered.

Crack!

More fractures spread across the shell as the little one kept pushing against it with its head.

Finally—

The eggshell gave way, shattering completely.

And from within, a tiny figure emerged, still wearing half a piece of shell like a little helmet.

"Yoji…"

Larvitar slowly opened its red-rimmed eyes, gazing at Johan's face in its reflection.

Then, it let out a soft call.

Seeing this, Johan finally relaxed.

Good. It looked perfectly healthy.

Larvitar reached out, broke off a piece of eggshell, and stuffed it into its mouth.

Crunch. Crunch—

But this hatching was destined to be anything but peaceful.

Just as Johan, Cynthia, and the others were enjoying the moment, a sudden sound shattered the tranquility.

"Shaaa!!"

Leaves rustled violently as a figure leaped from the forest.

It was a Pokémon—its body dark yellow, resembling a mantis, wielding two massive black battle axes.

Its claws, shoulders, and facial "fur" were made of jagged stone.

Those two enormous axes gleamed coldly in the light, and its expression was eerily serious—like a warrior standing guard at the final gate.

Johan's eyes widened in surprise, while Cynthia, besides her shock, was mostly confused.

Because—

This was a Pokémon she had never seen before!

Cleaver!

"Species unknown! Species unknown! Rotom error—!!"

Rotom Pokédex immediately crashed.

Cleaver—the alternative evolution of Scyther.

A Pokémon from the Hisui region. Bug/Rock-type.

By all logic, this species should have gone extinct long ago.

Yet, here one was, right before their eyes!

Whoosh—

Cleaver raised one of its massive, battle-worn axes, pointing it directly at Johan and Cynthia.

It was clear—if they took another step forward, it would attack.

The more nicks and cracks on a Cleaver's axes, the more experienced it was in battle—meaning its blades had only gotten sharper over time.

And based on the earlier sounds…

The Golem rockslide was definitely orchestrated by this Cleaver.

"What is that Pokémon?" Cynthia couldn't help but ask.

"Yoji!"

Suddenly, before anyone could answer, a small green blur shot past Johan and Cynthia.

A moment later—

Cleaver's raised axe had a tiny green Pokémon hanging from it.

Larvitar had latched onto the blade, biting down hard.

Scrrrrch!

A sharp, grating sound rang out as Larvitar's sharp teeth dug into the axe's edge.

"Shaaa (⊙_⊙)??"

Not just Johan and Cynthia—even Cleaver itself looked completely dumbfounded.

What… what is this thing?!

This axe had been tempered through countless battles! Just because it had cracks didn't mean it was weak!

This was a warrior's blade—tough and unyielding—

Crack!!

Just as that thought formed, Cleaver felt it.

A shudder ran through its axe.

The sound of something… breaking.

And there it was—

A massive, bite-shaped chunk missing from the blade.

Larvitar, meanwhile, had fallen back onto the ground, happily chewing on the chunk of rock.

"Shaaa (⊙△⊙)??"

Cleaver was stunned.

Johan and Cynthia were stunned.

Even the Pokémon were stunned.

Cynthia suddenly giggled, covering her mouth.

"Such a brave little one."

Brave?

Johan's mouth twitched.

That wasn't bravery—

That was gluttony!

A reckless little fool! A walking stomach!

Wasn't that "Uxie's Claw" on its head supposed to increase its intelligence?!

Where's the wisdom, huh?!

"Shaaa!!"

Cleaver finally snapped out of it, enraged.

With a furious roar, it swung its other axe straight at Larvitar.

Johan's expression darkened. "Gengar!"

"Ka-kaah~!"

Gengar grinned wickedly, flickered forward, and locked eyes with Cleaver.

And just like that—

Cleaver collapsed into the dirt.

Fast asleep.

Johan walked up and scooped Larvitar into his arms.

Even as a newborn, Larvitar was already heavy.

"Yoji?"

Larvitar blinked, puffing out its cheeks as if chewing something hard—yet still refusing to spit it out.

Recognizing Johan, its eyes brightened.

It reached out its tiny hands for a hug.

This little one looked just like a mini dinosaur, with a rough greenish-gray body, a single blunt horn on its head, and a small wagging tail.

"Larvitar, the Rock Skin Pokémon. Rock/Ground-type. The baby form of Tyranitar. It has a voracious appetite, eating constantly while awake. It is said to be able to devour an entire mountain, Rotom!"

Johan's face twitched.

…Yeah, yeah, no need to remind me.

Crunch, crunch—

Larvitar happily finished off the axe blade.

Johan sighed.

So this was his new teammate…

Johan placed Larvitar back on the ground.

Cynthia crouched beside him, her golden eyes curving in amusement.

"A very cute little one."

…Cute?

Well, maybe.

As long as he ignored the inevitable food expenses.

Johan sighed deeply. "We should deal with that Cleaver first."

"Cleaver?"

Cynthia turned her gaze toward the unconscious Cleaver, which was still twitching slightly in its sleep.

Was it… dreaming about Larvitar?

"You know what this Pokémon is?"

Honestly, Cynthia had thought there were no Pokémon she wasn't familiar with.

"If I'm not mistaken, this is a Pokémon from the Hisui region—or rather, an evolution line from back then," Johan explained.

"Or it could be that an ancient Hisuian relic caused a Scyther to evolve into Cleaver here."

Cleaver required a Black Augurite to evolve from Scyther.

"The Hisui region…" Cynthia murmured.

She didn't question how Johan knew this.

After all, he even had knowledge of Regigigas, Dialga, and Palkia.

A single Cleaver? Not surprising.

But Cynthia had a hunch—

This Cleaver might be the key to a major breakthrough in her research.

Johan, on the other hand, had no intention of capturing it.

Especially considering the high chance that it would mysteriously disappear within a few days.

Gulp—

Larvitar finally swallowed the last chunk of Cleaver's axe.

"Yoji."

The little one looked down at its belly, patted it lightly…

Then, realizing it wasn't full yet, it cheerfully trotted toward its remaining eggshell.

And began crunching away.

The other Pokémon gathered around, watching with curiosity.

Eating rocks? Eating eggshells?

This little one… sure had a unique appetite.

"Yoji?"

Sensing the stares, Larvitar paused, looking up in confusion.

It glanced around at its new teammates, hesitating for a moment.

Then, as if making a grand decision—

"Yoji!"

Crack—!

It broke off a piece of eggshell, held it out, and grinned brightly.

"Yoji?"

You guys want some too?

# 402: Larvitar, Successfully Captured!

Gengar was curious about what Larvitar's eggshell might taste like, but none of the other Pokémon were tempted to take a bite. They all politely declined.

Seeing this, Larvitar didn't insist. In just a few bites, it had completely devoured the remaining eggshell fragments.

But given its appetite, a small eggshell was nowhere near enough. Soon, its little stomach let out a loud grumble.

The little one wasn't clueless—it turned around and locked eyes on the unconscious Cleaver.

Fortunately, Johan quickly intervened.

That was not food.

A surprise bite was one thing, but eating too much… well, that could lead to bad habits.

Under Larvitar's confused gaze, Johan pulled out a box of Pokéblocks.

This immediately lifted the little one's spirits. It eagerly reached out with its tiny hands to accept the box and then—

It opened its mouth wide and swallowed the entire thing.

Johan: "…"

A loud crunch followed.

The sound of metal being chewed rang through the air.

Chomp, chomp, chomp—

With a gulp, Larvitar swallowed the whole box down.

"Yoji! (＞＜)☆"

Delicious!

Its bright red eyes twinkled with delight.

Pokéblocks tasted way better than rocks and eggshells!

"Cha."

Tinkaton nudged Gengar with her elbow.

Hey, this little one eats even more than I did!

Charcadet, meanwhile, was beaming with pride.

To it, a big appetite meant strong vitality and good health!

"Goka!"

See? The Pokémon I hatched is full of energy!

"Yoji…"

Larvitar scratched the dull spike on its head and gave a shy little grin.

Johan, however, was looking more and more concerned.

At this moment, he felt… as if the sky itself was collapsing on him.

"Pokéblocks are good, but Larvitar also needs to eat mineral-rich rocks to develop properly," Cynthia reminded him.

"Yeah…" Johan nodded.

He already knew this from Giovanni's Secrets of the Earth.

Pokéblocks provided essential nutrients, but without proper minerals, Larvitar's armor wouldn't harden properly, and its strength might suffer.

In short—

It needs EVERYTHING!

"Let's check out that ruin first," Johan said, handing Larvitar another box of Pokéblocks.

But this time, he opened the box himself and fed them one by one—just in case it tried to eat the container again.

"Alright." Cynthia stood up and headed toward the ruins.

Johan glanced at Larvitar again.

Actually, the moment the little one hatched, his Pokédex had already displayed its data—probably because it had instinctively used some moves while breaking the shell.

[Larvitar]

Type: Rock/Ground

Ability: Guts

Known Moves: Tackle, Leer, Rock Throw, Focus Energy, Pursuit, Dragon Dance

Mood: Happy, Excited (So many delicious things to eat!)

Condition: Excellent (This is a highly intelligent Larvitar!)

Friendship: 100 (Everything is great, just still a little hungry…)

Honestly, it had a lot of inherited moves. Clearly, Giovanni had chosen parents with excellent bloodlines.

No wonder both Professor Yamabuki and Elite Four Bertha had trouble determining its exact lineage.

After all, this Larvitar had even inherited Dragon Dance!

Johan could already picture it—

The moment Larvitar performed Dragon Dance in front of Garchomp…

Garchomp might not attack the little one, but he was definitely going to die.

Aside from that, Johan was more curious about the Pokédex's evaluation of Larvitar's status.

"A highly intelligent Larvitar?"

He glanced at the little one again.

Its tiny eyes were filled with nothing but hunger.

…Where was the intelligence in that?

"I swear, I need to have a word with Uxie about this."

Another box of Pokéblocks was quickly devoured.

"Yoji…"

Larvitar patted its belly.

Hmm… still only half full.

Half?

Its appetite was already rivaling Dragonite's and quickly catching up to Gengar's.

"Yoji!"

Suddenly, Larvitar's tiny nose twitched. Its eyes lit up, and it began waddling toward a rocky wall at the edge of the basin.

Once there, it started calling out excitedly.

"Oowoo?"

Ninetales tilted its head in confusion.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar signaled everyone to wait and then phased into the ground.

Moments later, it reemerged and nodded toward Ninetales.

Ninetales understood and immediately started digging with its Dig move.

Seeing Ninetales dig so skillfully, Larvitar eagerly joined in with its tiny hands.

Slowly but surely, its movements became more precise and faster.

"Wait… is it learning Dig on the spot?" Johan thought, his expression twitching.

Sure enough, the moment Larvitar surfaced with Ninetales, his Pokédex updated.

[New Move Learned: Dig]

"It already mastered Dig just like that?!"

Yes, Larvitar had a natural talent for burrowing, but this was too fast!

However, what shocked Johan even more was the item they uncovered—

A black, glossy rock.

It had a smooth, glass-like surface, resembling obsidian at first glance.

"Is this… Black Augurite?" Johan was taken aback.

[Black Augurite: Sharp and brittle when shattered, resembling both glass and obsidian. Scyther and Cleaver are particularly drawn to it.]

It was!

The very item required to evolve Scyther into Cleaver!

And Larvitar had just dug it up instinctively?!

"Cha~!"

Tinkaton swung her hammer.

Oh, I know this method well!

She often teamed up with Gengar and Ninetales to dig up metal.

Only this time, instead of metal, they'd found a rare evolution stone.

Johan had the same realization.

Did we just get a new excavation team member?

Larvitar filled the gap in Tinkaton's skillset—

From now on, no mineral, whether magnetic or non-magnetic, would be left behind.

They wouldn't just pick the bones clean—they'd consume the bones themselves.

"We're one step away from mining the entire planet."

"Yoji!"

Excited by its discovery, Larvitar chomped down on the Black Augurite.

Crunch!

A crisp sound echoed as it managed to break off a tiny piece.

It immediately sat down and started gnawing on it, humming a little tune.

"Wait… this is actually kinda catchy."

Cynthia was equally surprised.

"This island has Black Augurite?"

Johan rubbed his chin.

Yes, in the Legends: Arceus era, Cleavers were known to drop Black Augurite—but that was ancient history.

He glanced at the unconscious Graveler and Golem scattered across the ground.

"Ninetales, put a small piece back."

The Pokémon looked confused.

"If we leave some, maybe it'll 'grow' back over time."

Larvitar immediately nodded in agreement—then bit off another chunk.

Johan crouched down and placed a hand on Larvitar's head.

The little one looked up, blinking.

It recognized Johan instantly and beamed, reaching out for a hug.

Johan smiled.

"I have a gift for you."

He took out the purple scarf he had bought earlier.

"Yoji!"

Larvitar's eyes sparkled. It loved it.

Johan gently tied the scarf around its neck.

"Yoji?"

"It looks great," Johan said warmly.

Gengar and the others all gathered around to admire it.

Larvitar looked at its reflection in Rotom's screen and clenched its tiny fists in excitement.

I'll work hard for Johan and the team!

It reached out and tapped the Luxury Ball.

Click.

Captured instantly.

The team cheered.

A new partner had officially joined them!

Bzzt—!

Larvitar didn't stay inside the Luxury Ball for long. Almost immediately, it popped back out, hugging the Black Augurite as it happily continued gnawing on it.

Johan sighed.

"Of course, it would come right back out. Why did I even expect anything different?"

With Larvitar now officially part of the team, Johan had a new challenge—how to properly raise it.

Would it be compatible with Ninetales? What about the team's dynamics?

And most importantly—

"Can I even afford to feed it?"

That last thought hit him hard.

Feeding Larvitar = Working more jobs.

"Maybe if I just pick up a few extra side gigs…"

# 403: For the Sake of Food!

"How is it? Have you found anything?"

After capturing the Larvitar, Johan also arrived at the ruins.

The ruins were covered with various patterns—some were simple illustrations, while others represented text.

"Hmm."

Cynthia wore a serious expression. "I recognize some of the symbols, but most of them will take time to translate."

What was recorded here was likely related to the ancient Hisui period.

"Rotom, please record this for me," Cynthia suddenly said.

"Understood, Roto!"

Rotom instinctively responded.

Huh?

Rotom and Johan exchanged a glance, only now realizing what had just happened.

"Roto (O_o)??"

Suzuran Island, Contestant Village

"Rotom, Berry Blender Mode!"

"Roto!"

The high-end Berry Blender immediately started operating with a rhythmic hum.

"Yoji~"

Larvitar scratched its blunt horn, looking a little embarrassed but clearly yearning for the Berry Blender as it rubbed its belly.

That's right—it was hungry again.

Since returning from Dandelion Island, the curious Larvitar had been exploring and nibbling at everything in sight. It played for a while, got exhausted, and then became hungry.

Johan had no choice but to start making Pokéblocks.

The ones he had fed Larvitar earlier were from his old stock—delicious and highly nutritious, no doubt.

However, those Pokéblocks were mainly prepared to establish friendly relations with unfamiliar Pokémon, such as Darkrai, Cresselia, and Heatran.

Johan jokingly called them "Succubus Pokéblocks" because they were designed to be irresistibly tasty and adaptable to any Pokémon's palate.

That meant they weren't specifically tailored for Larvitar.

So, after returning, he immediately started making a custom batch.

Fortunately, The Secrets of the Earth had detailed notes from Giovanni on the type of Pokéblocks suitable for Larvitar.

With some of his own adjustments, Johan believed the final product would not only aid Larvitar's growth but also satisfy its taste buds.

Johan hadn't fully mapped out a training plan for Larvitar yet, but he had some ideas.

Larvitar was a Rock/Ground-type Pokémon, but its final evolution, Tyranitar, was Rock/Dark. That transition needed special attention.

Its primary training direction had to be physical attack-based. A slightly slower speed wouldn't be an issue since it could control the weather, but its offensive power had to be overwhelming.

Additionally, Larvitar's ability would eventually become Sand Stream upon evolving into Tyranitar.

Weather-based abilities could potentially conflict with Ice Ninetales' Snow Warning.

"…There might be a way to merge snow and sand into hail…"

As Johan pondered this while making Pokéblocks—

"Cha~"

Tinkaton, acting like a big sister, gently patted Larvitar on the head, nearly knocking it over.

But Larvitar wasn't upset; instead, it looked up at Tinkaton with a goofy grin.

Tinkaton sneakily glanced at Johan before taking out a shimmering silver piece of metal.

Larvitar blinked in surprise before eagerly opening its mouth—

—And chomped down on the metal, producing a crunching sound.

"Yoji! (`)"

"Cha!"

Wow! It really can eat that!

At that, the other Pokémon gathered around.

"Goka?"

Charcadet pulled out a small piece of charcoal and hesitantly offered it to Larvitar.

Without hesitation, Larvitar bit down—

"Yoji! (!!`)∑"

Only to spit it out immediately, sticking out its tongue and frantically fanning its mouth.

Hot!!

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar, now sporting a pair of glasses, rubbed its chin like a detective and made a conclusion:

Larvitar's favorite food was definitely rocks!

Hearing this—

"Draaa?"

Dragonite pulled out a greenish stone fragment, faintly glowing.

"Goka!"

Charcadet retrieved a dark red, somewhat dull stone that shimmered slightly.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar clapped its hands and spit out a fragment of the large Platinum Gem they had found yesterday, placing it in front of Larvitar.

Seeing this, Kubfu looked around anxiously, realizing it had nothing valuable to offer. In the end, it pitifully pulled out an Energy Root.

"Ku…"

Don't look down on it, okay?

Even Ice Ninetales only had a Never-Melt Ice to contribute.

"Yoji?"

Larvitar stared wide-eyed at the offerings before it, swallowing nervously.

Are… are these really for me?

Can I really eat them?

"Ka-kaah! (_)"

Gengar confidently patted Larvitar's head and waved a hand grandly.

Eat up! We're not stingy Pokémon!

"Yoji!! (ˊ〇ˋ*)"

Larvitar lifted its head, letting out an adorably fierce cry.

Surprisingly, it did look a bit like a "beast" in that moment.

Hearing the commotion, Johan turned around and immediately twitched.

Oh great.

They're actually feeding it this stuff, and it's actually eager to eat it!

Doesn't it hurt its teeth?!

"Put it down! All of you, put it down!" Johan quickly stepped in. "You can't just feed the little guy whatever you find! What if it gets indigestion?"

"And you—Marshadow—keep your little stick to yourself!"

"Marsha…"

The shadowy Marshadow awkwardly retreated, pouting in dissatisfaction.

What a waste. Doesn't even recognize the World Tree's branches…

Johan's mouth twitched.

That's exactly why I'm stopping you.

Feeding a Ground/Rock-type Pokémon a branch full of Grass-type energy? What kind of logic is that?

Do you not understand four times weakness to Grass?!

Even if it's imbued with the essence of life… you still shouldn't be randomly feeding it!

"Larvitar, don't just eat anything you see. You need to be a refined little—uh, a refined Larvitar."

Johan sat cross-legged and placed a freshly made Pokéblock in front of Larvitar.

"Yoji?"

A refined… Larvitar?

"Here, try this. It's good."

Larvitar happily began munching on it.

Johan sighed.

Its appetite was… something else.

"After this tournament, I'll have to order a batch of minerals…"

Thinking of minerals, Johan's first thought was naturally his student, Steven, since his family practically lived in mines.

However…

Steven's minerals were high-quality and expensive. Given his personality, he might even send them over for free, taking a loss in the process.

"I can't just keep mooching off Steven—it's not right as a teacher."

Looks like a trip to Oreburgh City was in order.

Oreburgh City also produced high-quality minerals. With both sources combined, he should be able to meet Larvitar's needs.

Larvitar could eat a variety of things—high-grade soil, rocks, coal, metal…

If you dared to feed it, it dared to eat.

That said…

According to Pokémon-world diets, Pidgey ate Caterpie, Weavile hunted Alolan Vulpix…

Larvitar's menu probably included Roggenrola, Geodude, Carbink, Rolycoly, and even Minior.

Johan quickly shook his head and dismissed the thought.

At most… just fallen fragments!

"…I wonder if Pokémon-world has uranium ore?"

If it did, feeding Larvitar some might turn it into Godzilla.

No matter what—

"This Suzuran Conference is going to be serious."

Johan's gaze sharpened.

"For the sake of food!"

"Ka-kaah!"

Led by Gengar, the Pokémon all cheered.

For the sake of food!

The "Outlaw Squad" had officially set their goal—Operation Food Supply had begun!

Hearing this, Larvitar wiped its mouth and let out an adorably fierce cry:

"Yoji!"

For the sake of food!

Viridian City, Rocket HQ

"Oh? It hatched? A Larvitar, huh…"

It was rare for Giovanni to receive a call from Johan.

Upon learning that the Pokémon hatched from the egg was a Larvitar, his mood improved significantly.

Raising a Larvitar and running out of money?

Running out of money was exactly the point!

Ideally, Johan would remain tied to the Rocket "war chariot," ensuring he could never leave.

This outcome was an unexpected but welcome surprise for Giovanni.

"It's a highly promising Pokémon. My suggestion is to build another Sandstorm team! Tell me what Pokémon you need—I should have the offspring of any Sandstorm team members." Giovanni spoke boldly.

Johan: "…"

So, Giovanni, was your plan all along to send me one Larvitar per year until I had a full Sandstorm team?

Thanks, I guess?

"Ahem, Gym Leader Giovanni, I wanted to ask—aside from what's mentioned in The Secrets of the Earth, is there anything else I should focus on when training Larvitar?"

"Focus, huh…"

Giovanni paused in thought before answering:

"I didn't hold anything back in The Secrets of the Earth. Everything I know is in there. As for Larvitar or its final evolution, Tyranitar, my only advice is… fully develop their offensive capabilities!"

After a brief pause, he added:

"Offense is the best defense. While that might not apply to every Pokémon, believe me—it absolutely applies to Tyranitar!"

Giovanni seemed to be getting excited.

"Relentless offense is equivalent to impenetrable defense. You must trust in the power of the earth!"

"The moment you stand firmly on the ground, you become invincible!"

Johan was speechless.

You and I are not the same, Giovanni!

Isn't the reason you and Mewtwo didn't get along because it prefers to float and fight from the air?

"But Johan, remember one thing—The Secrets of the Earth outlines the training method that suited me best. It may not be a perfect fit for you. You need to make your own decisions."

To be fair, Giovanni did have a master's attitude when it came to passing down knowledge.

"I understand."

Johan had received guidance from many people, but he had never blindly copied any of their training methods.

He firmly believed that no two Pokémon were ever exactly alike.

"Good. I trust your abilities. So…"

"Do your best in the Suzuran Conference."

"Haha." Johan chuckled dryly.

For the sake of food, I don't really have a choice but to do my best.

"If you need rare minerals, just let me know. Recently, Viridian Gym acquired a few more mining rights, and we've got a lot of extra miners working. We should be able to dig up quite a few valuable ores."

"Oh?" Johan's eyes lit up. "And the cost?"

"Don't worry about the cost."

Giovanni chuckled. "You can buy on credit!"

# 404: The Lily of the Valley Conference Begins!

On January 18th, the venue for the Lily of the Valley Conference was packed with people.

Today was the opening ceremony of the tournament!

Brilliant fireworks bloomed in the sky above the massive stadium, while hot air balloons featuring Pokémon like Pelipper and Wailord floated overhead, broadcasting the event live.

Amid the excited crowd, the host stepped onto the stage and announced the ceremonial lighting of the sacred flame.

"Goka~"

Charcadet held onto Larvitar, preventing it from running around while curiously observing the silver torch's flame.

After all, this was its first time attending a regional league tournament, and the spectacle was overwhelming.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar patted Charcadet's shoulder nonchalantly, indicating that the flame was just from Moltres and nowhere near as impressive as the fire on its own head.

"Goka."

Charcadet suddenly understood.

No wonder it seemed familiar…

"Seriously, can't the League get Ho-Oh's flames? They use Moltres' fire every time," Johan couldn't help but comment.

The host took the stage, microphone in hand, and announced, "Next, let's welcome President Damalanche for his speech!!"

The old man kept it brief, simply expressing his support for the Lily of the Valley Conference.

It made sense—since every region held a tournament every year, if Damalanche gave a long-winded speech at each one, people would probably get sick of it.

"I don't see any connection to Tobias…" Johan muttered, shaking his head.

"Tobias?" Cynthia looked at him with curiosity.

"Ah, just some card player."

"Bertha is taking the stage!" Flint said excitedly.

Haven't you seen her before…?

The cheers from the crowd grew even louder. Compared to President Damalanche, the Elite Four clearly held more weight in the audience's eyes.

"Johan, finally found you."

A familiar voice called out.

Johan turned toward it.

"Professor Oak… and Samson Oak?"

Standing before him were two nearly identical elderly men, differentiated only by their hairstyles and skin tones.

Oh, and by their outfits—Professor Oak wore his signature white lab coat, while Samson Oak was dressed in Alolan beachwear and flip-flops.

Isn't it cold on Lily of the Valley Island? Johan couldn't help but wonder.

"Alola, Professor Johan, Ninetales!"

Samson Oak greeted Johan with a smile, and the next second, his hair transformed into the shape of Ninetales.

Johan: "…"

Cynthia: "…"

Flint: "Wow, that's amazing!"

As expected of the man known as "The One with a Thousand Pokémon Faces."

A considerable number of people from Alola had come to this Lily of the Valley Conference.

Compared to regions like Kanto and Johto, Sinnoh was relatively new in terms of League establishment, though its system was already quite refined.

Alola, on the other hand, was still in the early stages of forming its own League.

Participating in this tournament would not only help spread awareness of Alola and boost its economy but also provide a chance to learn from Sinnoh and other regions on how to establish a more structured League system.

Of course, Samson Oak's primary reason for seeking out Johan was—

"Hahaha! Our Alola Pokémon Academy would be more than happy to welcome you, Johan. Gengar!"

Samson Oak got straight to the point during their brief exchange.

He wasn't necessarily trying to get Johan to "jump ship" to the Alola Pokémon Academy—he just wanted him to visit and conduct an exchange program.

After all, whether it was Professor Kukui or students like Lana and Mallow, they all greatly admired Johan.

"If I get the chance, I'd love to," Johan responded earnestly without rejecting the idea.

Who wouldn't like sunshine, beaches, beautiful women, and bikinis?

Especially if Cynthia came along…

Cough.

"By the way, Johan, hand me your Pokédex. I'll upgrade it for you while I'm here," Professor Oak said.

"Rotom."

"Understood, Roto!"

Rotom immediately emerged from the Pokédex and flew out, leaving the device in Professor Oak's hands.

Meanwhile…

After Bertha's speech, the host announced loudly:

"The Lily of the Valley Conference officially begins!"

Fireworks exploded across the sky. Excited trainers shouted, and the audience's cheers reached a fever pitch.

Who knew what kind of thrilling stories would unfold in this year's Sinnoh League tournament?

With the opening ceremony concluded, the preliminary rounds began immediately.

Trainers in the preliminaries would battle fiercely throughout the day, and only the top 64 would advance to the knockout stage.

As a special invitee, Johan didn't need to go through the preliminaries. So, after the ceremony, he spent time exploring the area with Cynthia and Flint.

Later, two late arrivals—Lucian and Aaron—finally reached Lily of the Valley Island.

The five of them gathered at a café.

"Yoji~"

Larvitar was brimming with curiosity about everything around it.

And for this little glutton and lovable fool, the best way to satisfy curiosity was—

Chomp!

"Ow, ow, ow, ow—"

Flint yelped in pain as he tried to tease Larvitar with a piece of cake, only for it to latch onto his hand, leaving him with half a limb's worth of regret.

Luckily, Charcadet quickly pried it off.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar observed the scene thoughtfully.

Could it be that this little dinosaur's best skill would be Pain-Induced Sleep?

"So the egg hatched into a Larvitar, huh?"

Lucian mused. "I've heard they need to eat an entire mountain before they evolve."

Johan took a sip of his coffee and smiled.

"Lucian, your information is incomplete. You might want to update it."

Then, he casually handed back the file Lucian had compiled on Pokémon Hunter J and her group.

Lucian: "???"

"Just kidding."

The file was actually quite decent. Most of the details were common knowledge, but it was clear Lucian had his own sources.

"Have you noticed that Hunter J's group has been appearing unusually frequently in Eterna City?" Johan asked, flipping through the file.

Lucian adjusted his glasses. "That's true. I've noticed it as well. But isn't that because of Eterna Forest?"

As one of Sinnoh's largest wild Pokémon habitats, it was natural for a Pokémon hunter like J to be active in the area.

If Lucian was looking at it purely from a logical standpoint, there wasn't anything unusual.

But for Johan, who had prior knowledge, leading things in the "right" direction was a simple matter.

"Then why don't they go through Floaroma Town?"

Lucian pondered for a moment and caught on.

"…I see. I'll keep an eye on Eterna City."

"Also, can you help me gather information on the other competitors in this tournament?" Johan asked.

"Sure."

Lucian agreed instinctively—then suddenly froze.

Wait.

Had he just signed himself up for another task without realizing it?

He opened his mouth, glanced at Johan's smiling face, then at Cynthia casually playing with Larvitar…

Nope. Not picking that fight.

Then he looked at Flint, who was still nursing his wounded arm, and Aaron, who was spacing out.

Not useful. Not useful at all.

Wait a second.

We're all supposed to be part of the same team, so why am I the only one doing actual work?

Where's the teamwork?

You all relax while I do all the work?!

"Wait, does Johan even need to research the competition?" Aaron suddenly spoke, his ahoge perking up.

"Yoji…"

Larvitar glanced at Aaron's ahoge, then touched its own blunt horn.

Hey! Is he copying me?

"Yeah, with Johan's strength, can't he just steamroll everyone?" Flint added.

"Johan: "I'm not Leon."

The group fell silent for a moment.

Leon's strategy was basically turn everything into a Charizard sweep.

Even Cynthia often relied on her Garchomp to bulldoze opponents.

In fact, many top-tier Champions excelled at this kind of overwhelming strategy.

Johan sighed and took a deep breath.

Anyone who stood in the way of feeding his Pokémon…

Would be crushed.

"Yeah," he said, nodding slightly.

# 405: This Strategy Is Called "Reckless"!

5 bonus chapter's coming out at 11 AM EST. So I ask that you save you're powerstones until then.

-----------

January 19th, early morning.

"Why are my dreams getting weirder again?"

Johan sat on his bed, deep in thought.

Last night, in his dream, Gengar suddenly shouted, "Henshin!" and the entire scene shifted into something bizarre.

He had to admit, though—Gengar looked pretty cool back then. That little red cape really gave it a card-art aesthetic.

But then, things got even weirder.

Gengar, now looking like something straight out of a trading card, grabbed a shoulder pole, teamed up with an Ninetales dressed as Snow White, and led a gang—including Tinkaton, Dragonite, Kubfu, Charcadet, and Larvitar—to start moving mountains.

And then... a whole mining mountain just took off.

"This has to be because I just caught Larvitar. Thinking about it all day has made me dream about it at night... I'm under way too much stress."

Johan let out a long sigh.

Other people made high-grade Pokéblocks and enjoyed a steady stream of income.

Him? He was barely managing to keep his little food-loving team fed.

"Alright, time to get up."

He threw off his blanket and hugged Ninetales, giving it a few firm rubs until the little one let out a slightly disgruntled whimper. Only then did Johan, grinning, get out of bed.

The first thing he did was check on Larvitar.

He found the little one curled up inside a container that looked like a Poké Ball—a mix between a cup and a bowl. Seeing this, he sighed in relief.

Thank goodness. At least it hadn't started chewing on the house yet. No random bite marks, no missing bricks.

After all, a refined Larvitar baby couldn't just eat anything.

That said, the "emergency food supply" he'd prepared in the container was completely wiped out overnight.

On the bright side...

That red-and-white ball-like container had become both Larvitar's food bowl and its bed. That saved some money, right?

Noticing Johan's gaze, Larvitar rubbed its eyes, stretched its tiny body, and let out a long yawn.

"Yoji~ (ˊ〇ˋ*)"

The Hungry Dragon Roars!

Great. One blink, and another pile of food was gone.

But there was nothing he could do.

Larvitar was just too cute.

"Alright, little one, time to get up."

"Yoji!"

Larvitar nodded, wiggled its little hands, flailed its tiny legs, and wobbled out of its "bed-bowl"—only to faceplant onto the floor with a plop.

The little guy, however, didn't seem to feel any pain at all. It just stood up, patted the dust off its backside, and stared expectantly at Johan.

No need to say a word—the meaning was clear.

Hungry!

Johan's eye twitched.

"First, brush your teeth. Clean teeth mean sharp teeth. Sharp teeth mean better eating."

Larvitar tilted its head, somewhat understanding.

As for teaching the little guy how to brush its teeth? That job was going to Gengar, who also had a solid set of chompers.

Aside from Ninetales, practically all of Johan's Pokémon had been personally trained by Gengar in the art of dental hygiene. It had plenty of experience.

Right on cue, Gengar had just returned from its morning run with Dragonite.

"Gengar, the toothbrushing job's on you."

"Ka-kaah! (＞Д＜)ゝ"

Gengar saluted.

Mission accepted!

But moments later, from the bathroom—

"Ka-kaah! (The toothbrush is gone!)"

"Ka-kaah! (The toothpaste is gone!)"

"Ka-kaah! (The cup is gone!)"

"...Ka-kaah. (The whole sink…)"

Johan smacked his forehead.

The Suzuran Conference – Grass Field

Johan was assigned to the grass battlefield. A big screen displayed the matchups, and the arena was already buzzing with excitement.

Among the crowd were plenty of Johan's fans—though their true intentions were questionable.

But as Johan often said, "Haters are still fans. Better a hater than a turncoat."

Whether it was the organizers' doing or just luck, Johan's match was scheduled first. Even before they entered the field, the audience was already roaring.

"Professor Johan! A real man doesn't need a second Pokémon!"

"We want to see strategy! It's been too long since we've seen strategy!"

"Why isn't there an unlimited battle format? Let Johan fight too!"

Clearly, most of the cheers were just hyping things up.

Johan chuckled. Come on, he wasn't Ash—what was this about unlimited battles?

If Ash got an unlimited format, he wouldn't even need to call out his Pokémon. Pikachu could just keep the show going forever.

"Wait... why does the name 'Dino' sound familiar?"

Flipping through the info Fitzwilliam had gathered, Johan thought for a moment.

Then it hit him.

Wasn't this guy the runner-up of the Ever Grande Conference the year Ash competed?

He had lost to Basil and his "Eevee Team" in the finals.

But that was still a few years away. Right now, Dino was still relatively inexperienced.

According to Fitzwilliam's research, he had only participated in the Johto League's Silver Conference once, where he placed in the Top 32.

"Running into someone familiar in Round One? Not a bad start."

"Now, let's welcome our two Trainers!"

The announcer's voice boomed through the stadium.

As the two Trainers walked onto the field from opposite tunnels, Johan remained calm, while Dino looked visibly tense.

He was wearing a black vest and a gray sleeveless coat, his fists clenched, expression serious.

His opponent was the famous "Baby Cup Slayer," Professor Johan—strength unknown, but always rising to the challenge.

Dino took a deep breath, forcing himself to stay composed.

No matter what...

At the very least, he had to show Professor Johan that he was no "baby"!

Dino's eyes sharpened with determination.

"Nice look. I like this kid," Flint said from the stands.

Fitzwilliam shot him a glance, adjusting his glasses with a smirk.

"You had an even sharper gaze when you first challenged Professor Johan."

"Huh? Did I? Hahahaha—"

Flint scratched his head awkwardly before quickly adding, "Next time! Next time I challenge Professor Johan, I'll definitely win!"

He sounded completely confident—probably because Johan wasn't there to hear him.

Click.

Fitzwilliam pressed a button on a recording device, waved it in front of Flint, and smiled meaningfully.

"You sneaky—! Fitzwilliam, you're so dirty!" Flint's eyes widened.

Fitzwilliam, on the other hand, looked unfazed. "Just a professional habit I've been developing."

"Bro~" Flint admitted defeat.

Cynthia simply smiled, staying out of their squabble.

On the Battlefield

"Trainers, send out your Pokémon!"

Dino tossed his Poké Ball. "Go, Chandelure!"

The chandelier-shaped Pokémon emerged, its main body bearing two yellow eyes. Wisps of eerie purple flames flickered atop its head and along its curved arms.

Johan, without hesitation, threw out his own Poké Ball.

"It's you, Charcadet!"

"Goka!!"

During the previous Indigo Conference, Johan hadn't yet caught this little one. But for the Suzuran Conference, Charcadet had eagerly volunteered to take the lead.

Not only to experience tournament battles, but also to show Larvitar what fighting the "Johan way" looked like.

Seeing Charcadet, Dino furrowed his brows slightly.

He had watched some of Johan's battles, especially from the Indigo Conference, but there wasn't much information on Charcadet.

"Battle, begin!"

At the referee's signal, Dino made the first move.

"Chandelure, Trick Room!"

A black field rapidly expanded outward from Chandelure, covering the entire battlefield.

"Alright! Straight into Trick Room—this one's playing the strategy game!"

"But Johan sent out Charcadet, so no Will-O-Wisp next turn!"

"Wait... are we sure Chandelure is actually slower than Charcadet?"

"!!"

That last question was a sharp one.

In the games, Chandelure had a base Speed of 80, while Charcadet had only 35. Trick Room would normally work against Charcadet.

But in the real world, base stats weren't everything.

Johan simply smiled.

To be honest, this Trick Room didn't affect this battle much...

But for the next one? That was another story.

"Charcadet, Astonish!"

As Chandelure cast Trick Room, Charcadet had already charged forward, flames flaring up, standing out against the black field.

Since he sent out Charcadet, Johan wasn't going to play strategy.

Or rather—his strategy was simple:

Go. All. In.

"Goka!!"

Its unwavering, aggressive stance made Chandelure's eyes shrink as it instinctively backed up.

"Flare Blitz!"

The moment Chandelure flinched, Johan seized the opportunity.

Blazing flames erupted around Charcadet as it slammed into Chandelure.

Dino gasped. "Chandelure, Shadow Ball!"

As a Fire/Ghost-type Pokémon, Chandelure wasn't taken down in one hit. Enduring Charcadet's flames, it quickly gathered a swirling mass of dark energy.

Boom!

At such close range, there was no way to dodge.

But Charcadet didn't even flinch. Taking the hit head-on, it raised both arms high.

Its glowing red arms and the flickering flames in its eyes showed its rising battle spirit.

"Flamethrower!"

Fwoosh!

In an instant, golden-tinged flames surged forth, engulfing Chandelure.

The flames scattered in all directions, and when they faded—

Chandelure lay on the ground, eyes swirling.

"Goka!!"

Charcadet raised its fists triumphantly, the fire around it burning even brighter as its vitality surged.

"Woah—this is a whole new side of Professor Johan! This battle style…"

"It's so reckless, but I love it!"

"No, no, you guys don't get it! That wasn't 'Flamethrower'—that was Overheat! Seriously!"

"On the outside, he's just charging in, but inside, he's totally calculated—yep, that's still the Professor Johan we know!"

Dino's expression twitched.

He never expected that the famous Professor Johan—renowned for his tactical prowess—wouldn't be playing the strategy game today.

It felt like punching a pillow.

You try playing aggressively, he goes for strategy.

You try playing strategy, he goes all-in aggressive.

And the worst part?

No matter which style he played—he still won.

But at least…

One Flare Blitz plus a Shadow Ball—Charcadet's condition should be pretty bad by now… right?

# 406: As Dirty as Ever, Professor Johan!

5 bonus chapter's coming out at 11 AM EST. So I ask that you save you're powerstones until then.

-----------

Chandelure losing the battle made Dino truly feel Johan's strength.

However, he wasn't discouraged. The Trick Room was still active, and Charcadet wasn't in great condition.

So…

After recalling Chandelure, Dino wasted no time. While Trick Room was still in effect, he quickly threw out his second Poké Ball.

"Go, Druddigon!"

It was the so-called "Disgrace of the Dragon Clan", Druddigon!

However…

Druddigon earned this title for two main reasons.

First, despite having wings, it couldn't fly—while even Garchomp could. That alone made it seem ridiculous.

Second, its base stat total was only 485.

Before Pokémon like Drampa and "Fruit Dragons" appeared, Druddigon had the lowest total base stats among all fully evolved Dragon-types.

But aside from its inability to fly, its Sheer Force ability made its physical attacks terrifyingly powerful. Its only real weakness was its low Speed.

But now… the Trick Room was in play!

That last weakness was completely covered.

"Oh, I get it! Chandelure's Trick Room was set up for Druddigon!"

"Dragon-types resist Fire moves. Plus, with Trick Room in play, is Professor Johan finally going to switch out his Pokémon?"

"Professor Johan, don't be afraid—just go for it!"

"…"

The battle continued!

"Druddigon, Rock Slide!"

"Garrr!!"

Druddigon let out a roar. A massive boulder materialized above Charcadet, forming so quickly that Charcadet had almost no room to dodge.

However, as a Pokémon switched into battle, even with Trick Room boosting its speed, Druddigon still couldn't move before Charcadet—who was already on the field and prepared.

So…

Before Druddigon could act, Charcadet had already hurled a small orange fireball.

It wasn't big, but it was fast. The key was—

Boom!!

The fireball exploded upon contact with Druddigon. It didn't deal much damage, but it left behind faint flickering embers.

Burn status!

Druddigon's attack power took an unavoidable hit due to the burn, drastically weakening its physical attacks!

Only after that did the boulder finally crash down.

Bang!!

The massive rock shattered upon impact with Charcadet, breaking apart into several pieces.

At the same time, the green barrier surrounding Charcadet—Protect—also shattered!

Moves like Protect had priority and weren't affected by Trick Room.

"This time, let's see you block this! Druddigon, Stomping Tantrum!" Dino called out confidently.

To be safe, Dino chose a high-accuracy move that also had area coverage.

With Sheer Force as its ability and, as Johan suspected, the Life Orb as its held item, even with the burn weakening its power, Stomping Tantrum was still a massive threat to Charcadet—who had a double weakness to Ground-type moves.

But Johan simply smirked.

Block? No need.

The earlier Protect was only to ensure that Will-O-Wisp landed properly—just in case his "luck" made it miss. Now that the burn had landed and Druddigon's Attack was lowered…

Fwoosh!!

The flames surrounding Charcadet flared up once more. Like a miniature locomotive, it charged forward, stomping through the tremors caused by Stomping Tantrum.

Flare Blitz!

Boom!

The battlefield erupted in a massive explosion, sending shockwaves across the ground.

Druddigon was knocked backward, its chest now scorched red!

Meanwhile, Charcadet seemingly disappeared into the thick dust cloud.

"So fearless! Even if it faints, it's making sure to deal as much damage as possible!"

"Didn't think I'd see Professor Johan use a second Pokémon so soon…"

"Wait! Look!"

The dust obstructed the audience's view.

And it wasn't just the audience—Dino also struggled to see clearly.

In his mind, Charcadet must have fainted from the recoil of Flare Blitz, combined with the damage from Stomping Tantrum, right?

But before the dust could settle, a fiery red glow flickered within it.

Dino's face changed dramatically. "Druddigon, Sludge Bomb!"

Whoosh—

But before Druddigon could act, a gust of Heat Wave blasted forward, instantly dispersing the dust cloud—right into Druddigon's face.

Heat Wave!

And not just any Heat Wave—it came in surging waves, carrying an odd special energy that made each blast stronger than the last.

Sizzle—

The tiny flames flickering on Druddigon's burned body suddenly flared up.

"Garrr!!"

The scorching heat overwhelmed Druddigon. Its entire body turned bright red, and, unable to withstand the pain, its eyes rolled back as it collapsed.

Druddigon is unable to battle!

Meanwhile, Charcadet was still standing—albeit panting, hands on its knees.

"Wait, what does this Charcadet even eat? How does it have so much stamina?"

Simple.

It was just… eating some charcoal, the highest-grade Pokéblocks, and had absorbed a sliver of the Flame of Life.

Flint sneered from the audience. "Pfft. You guys are clueless. Ever heard of the Flame of Life?"

Conveniently forgetting how shocked he had been when he first learned about it himself.

"Cynthia, Johan's Charcadet hasn't evolved, but its level must be pretty high…"

Fitzwilliam stared at Charcadet, still struggling but standing. His expression turned strange.

"Actually, the scariest part is… I think Charcadet is faking it."

Flint blinked, then suddenly understood. "That makes sense! That's totally something Professor Johan would do!"

Buzz—

Trick Room wore off.

Now, Dino had truly lost any advantage.

But he didn't give up.

He was so close—just a little bit more!

Judging by Charcadet's current state, one more hit should be enough to take it down!

Defeating even one of Professor Johan's Pokémon would be a success!

Clenching his fists, Dino pumped himself up.

At the same time, he sent out his final Pokémon.

"Go, Mandibuzz!"

A large, vulture-like Pokémon with bone decorations took to the field.

"Going for a speed-focused approach now?"

Johan smirked. He knew exactly what Dino was thinking.

"Mandibuzz, Brave Bird!!"

This was it. Everything came down to this attack!

Dino's eyes burned with determination.

"Scraaa!!"

Mandibuzz flapped its wings, screeching loudly as it became cloaked in a blue aura, then dove down at Charcadet at high speed.

"Goka!"

At the last second, Charcadet suddenly straightened up. A golden flame flickered atop its head, and the fire around its body erupted into waves.

"Wait—I KNEW it was faking!" Flint yelled.

The audience gasped in shock.

In the next moment…

"Overheat!!"

BOOM!!

A roaring inferno exploded outward, engulfing the entire battlefield in flames.

The sheer heat was so intense that even the stadium's protective barriers flickered, barely holding up.

The front-row spectators could feel the heat through the barrier.

A thick column of smoke shot up through the open-air section of the stadium—like a volcanic eruption.

The crowd was speechless.

Wait… that was Overheat?

Where was the "Overheat" symbol?

Wasn't Charcadet supposed to be exhausted? How did it still unleash something this strong?!

When the flames cleared, Mandibuzz lay on the ground, charred black, unconscious.

"Goka~"

Charcadet let out a small breath of black smoke, standing still as the battlefield grew silent.

"I get it now! Professor Johan was pretending to be reckless! It was all deception! A complete mind game!"

"That's so dirty!"

"Going all out from the start—Professor Johan is here to take the championship!"

Johan's famous online comment resurfaced in everyone's minds—"I'll crush them all!"

And that's exactly what was happening.

"Winner: Johan!"

The stadium erupted in cheers.

"He's back! I can feel it—Professor Johan is back!"

"As dirty as ever, Professor Johan!"

Dino forced a smile, shaking Johan's hand. "I still have a long way to go…"

Johan patted his shoulder. "Believe in yourself. The future is yours!"

And this time, Johan wasn't just saying it.

Because one day, Dino would almost win the Ever Grande Conference.

He had potential—he just needed to keep pushing forward.

"Thank you, Professor Johan."

Dino's eyes shone with newfound determination.

# 407: Cake Battle—First Come, First Served! (BONUS)

"Yoji! Yoji, Yoji!"

Larvitar excitedly greeted Johan and Charcadet upon their return, congratulating them on their victory.

"Goka!"

Charcadet, acting like an elder, gently patted Larvitar's head.

Though, in reality, they were about the same height.

Johan's battle against Dino had ended, marking a fantastic start to the tournament. The crowd was still fired up from Charcadet's blazing performance.

Arriving at Cynthia's private suite—

"Congratulations, Professor Johan!" Flint was the first to offer his praise, followed by the others.

"It was just a Round of 64 match," Johan smiled, not making a big deal of it.

Though, since Flint was being so enthusiastic…

Next time Flint made a mistake, Johan would consider letting it slide.

With nothing urgent coming up, Johan stayed to watch the rest of the battles with them.

Down on the battlefield, Guzma stood with one hand in his pocket, his face wearing an arrogant smirk as he casually waved his hand.

"Pinsir, finish it—Guillotine!"

Pinsir viciously grabbed Swanna. At such close range, there was no chance for the attack to miss. The overwhelming force delivered devastating damage.

"The winner is Guzma!" the referee announced, raising the flag.

"Swanna!"

The opposing Trainer rushed onto the field, cradling their unconscious and heavily injured Swanna.

Guzma shot them a sideways glance, sneering.

"Battling is about destruction! If you're not prepared for that, then go home and cry!"

With that, he shoved both hands into his pockets and walked toward the exit without even bothering with the customary handshake.

"Weaklings—and weak Pokémon—don't deserve to shake hands with the great Guzma!"

The audience, though somewhat intimidated by Guzma's aura, remained mostly silent.

Meanwhile, a group of Team Skull members cheered wildly for him.

There was no denying it—Guzma's behavior had rubbed many people the wrong way.

Yet, there were still some spectators, drawn to the thrill of violence, who cheered him on.

After all, Guzma had just steamrolled through three opponents using only his Pinsir.

As the battles continued, none of the other matches ended in a similar one-sided sweep.

However, there were still plenty of interesting moments—

"The winner is Louis!"

Following the referee's announcement, the victorious burly man raised the umbrella in his hand and pointed it directly at Johan's suite.

His intention was crystal clear.

He was challenging Johan!

The entire stadium erupted in shock.

The last person who had openly challenged Johan on the tournament stage was Paul…

And in the end, Paul got absolutely demolished by Gengar.

"Are Trainers these days just this bold?" Flint murmured, swallowing nervously.

Fitzwilliam glanced at him.

Weren't you just as bold back then?

Actually—no.

Flint was even bolder.

He had challenged Cynthia over a hundred times.

Meanwhile, Johan was focused on something else.

The name "Louis"… sounded oddly familiar.

Wasn't he the guy who lost to Alain?

The one with a Garchomp?

"Professor Johan, aren't you going to react?" Flint asked.

"Hm?"

Johan snapped out of his thoughts and smiled.

"His courage is commendable."

The Sinnoh League sure knew how to put on a show.

On the first day, they had scheduled Johan's battle as the opening match to maximize hype.

If that had just been luck, then when the matchups for the Round of 32 were announced, it became very clear that the League was deliberately stirring up drama.

Johan vs. Louis.

The very same Louis who had challenged Johan on stage.

Just like that, the internet exploded.

"Round of 32 matchup—Professor Johan vs. 'The Umbrella-Wielding Louis'!"

"Oh ho ho! Damn, I cannot wait for this battle!"

"Which Pokémon do you think Professor Johan will use? Gengar? Ninetales? Dragonair?"

"My vote's on Gengar! No particular reason—I just wanna see someone's soul break."

"But what if—just what if—Professor Johan actually loses?"

"LMAO. That'd be even funnier!"

Online, the spectators were eating up the drama.

The bigger the spectacle, the better.

One thing was certain—

Both the Lily of the Valley Conference and Louis were raking in attention.

The Round of 32 and Round of 16, which usually didn't draw that much hype, were now skyrocketing in popularity.

Of course, Johan wasn't the only Trainer making waves.

This year's Lily of the Valley Conference had attracted many strong competitors.

The one Johan was paying the most attention to?

Tobias.

He, too, had effortlessly swept his opponent in a 3-0 victory.

And his lead Pokémon?

Darkrai.

A Mythical Pokémon—one of the very few ever seen in an official League tournament.

Instantly, Tobias became the center of attention.

Aside from him, another standout was the so-called "Emperor of Destruction," Guzma.

With his brash attitude, cocky demeanor, and ruthless battling style, he had cemented himself as a major figure in this tournament.

These three—Johan, Tobias, and Guzma—were receiving the most attention.

Other notable Trainers included:

Ka Wu, a seasoned veteran from Hoenn known as the Unyielding Flaming Fighter. Magua, a powerhouse from Galar dubbed the Crusher of Solid Rock.

Simply put—

This year's Lily of the Valley Conference had way more elite competitors than usual.

Fans online had reached a consensus:

"This tournament is way too stacked. Most people can't handle it!"

For Dino, however, this realization made him feel a lot better about his loss.

During an interview, he said:

"Professor Johan told me—he won today, but the future belongs to me!"

The reporter interviewing him hesitated before carefully pointing out:

"During the Indigo Conference, both Otani and Melanie also received that exact same compliment."

Dino: "???"

While the internet was buzzing with discussions—

What was Johan doing?

"Left—move it a little left—yes, perfect! Good job, Flint and Infernape."

Johan nodded in satisfaction.

In the League's commercial district, at a small shop near the edge of the area—

Flint's Infernape perched on Flint's shoulders, securing a sign above the stall:

Sunbeam Café!

Inside, a Indeedee duo and an Alcremie were busy setting up.

They carefully placed cakes and pastries—prepared earlier in Rustboro City—into the display case and on the counters.

"Great work, everyone," Johan said with a smile.

"Farrr~!"

"Fetch'd! (o(〃'▽'〃)o)"

"So this is what you meant by bringing them here—opening a temporary café!" Steven finally understood.

"Well, with the tournament's popularity soaring, why not?" Johan placed his hands on his hips, nodding in satisfaction.

He could already tell—

The hype for this year's tournament was only going to increase.

As for the Rustboro café?

It'd be temporarily closed.

Right now, he was here to cash in on the tournament traffic.

Johan had a feeling—

His café's coffee and pastries were about to sell out.

"Of course, it's not just about that."

Johan gently patted Indeedee on the head.

"I also promised them I'd do this."

"Farrr~!"

"Hm?" Steven was puzzled.

"Come on, let's grab some snacks. With Indeedee's baking skills and Alcremie's fresh cream—this stuff is incredible!"

Meanwhile—

Cynthia sat inside the café, staring at the Alcremie Cake in front of her, spoon in hand, hesitating.

It was so cute.

Could she really eat it?

Gengar, however, had zero hesitation.

It had already demonstrated the proper eating method—one bite at a time!

"Yoji!! (ω)"

Larvitar's eyes sparkled as it stared at the table full of pastries.

"The ones here are for us to eat freely," Johan said. "But everything made after this? Those are for selling."

Because if he let them eat as much as they wanted…

They wouldn't need a café.

They'd clear out the inventory in minutes.

"Draaa!!"

Dragonair cheered as it joined the feast.

Kubfu's fur was already covered in whipped cream—cleaning up later was going to be a huge task.

Ninetales, on the other hand, ate more gracefully. Yet, for some reason, its cake was disappearing just as fast as Tinkaton's.

"Goka!"

Just as Larvitar was about to dig in, Charcadet stopped it, looking serious.

We have to serve the guests first. That's proper hospitality!

"Yoji?"

Larvitar tilted its head, confused.

It then watched as Charcadet picked up a plate with a minty, cream-filled Alcremie Cake and carried it over to Flint and Infernape's table, carefully placing it in front of them.

Flint was nearly moved to tears.

"As expected of a Fire-type Pokémon! It knows who the real Fire-type expert is!"

See? Flint thought.

This Charcadet had personally delivered them a cake!

"Th-thank you…!"

But before he could finish his sentence—

Charcadet bowed politely, then promptly took the Mareep Wool Cake in front of Flint and the Boltund Hot Dog in front of Infernape and carried them back to Larvitar's table.

Now, Charcadet had the Mareep Wool Cake, while Larvitar had the Boltund Hot Dog.

Flint and Infernape looked at the single remaining cake on their table… then at the two dishes that had just been taken away.

At the same time, identical question marks appeared over their heads.

Flint & Infernape: "???"

"Yoji!! (ω)" Larvitar exclaimed.

I get it now!

"Goka~" Charcadet calmly motioned with its hands.

No need to be surprised—this is just normal procedure.

Infernape gave Flint a side-eye.

"Ku? (￢_￢)"

This is your so-called 'Fire-type affinity' huh?

Flint opened his mouth, but he had nothing to say.

All he could do was sigh internally.

Professor Johan's Pokémon really are something else…

"Eat quickly before Gengar devours everything," Johan reminded Cynthia and Steven.

This was a cake battle—first come, first served!

"This cake is so cute… I almost don't want to eat it," Cynthia murmured.

"Don't worry. Indeedee and Alcremie can always make more!" Johan reassured her with a grin.

"Farrr~"

Indeedee proudly presented another tray of desserts.

Ta-da! A brand-new creation—Gengar Chocolate Pudding!

Cynthia's eyes immediately lit up.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar, its face covered in whipped cream, suddenly felt a chill run down its spine.

It looked up, only to see Cynthia slicing a Gengar-shaped pudding in half with her spoon.

Then, it turned its gaze toward Larvitar.

The little dragon's expression seemed to ask—

"Is that pudding… the real Gengar?"

"Ka-kaah…"

Gengar gulped.

# 408: A Polite One-Hit KO! (BONUS)

January 20th, clear skies.

At the Lily of the Valley Conference, the matches to determine the Top 16 from the Top 32 were underway.

The atmosphere was electric, with every seat taken and the crowd roaring with excitement.

Cynthia sat in her seat, nibbling on a plump Gengar-themed cake.

"Boss lady hasn't had ice cream in a while, has she?" Aaron's ahoge perked up as if he had made a groundbreaking discovery.

Flint crossed his legs. "As Johan-sensei would say, 'It's winter. Cut back on ice cream and drink more hot water.'"

"But doesn't eating too much cake make you gain weight?" Bertha questioned, puzzled. "If boss lady gets fat—"

He puffed up his cheeks to mimic someone gaining weight, his mind already picturing a chubby Cynthia. Instantly, he felt a chill down his spine.

He stiffly turned his head.

Aaron and Flint had already bolted to Steven's side without him noticing. Aaron even gave him a subtle thumbs-up.

Resting on Bertha's shoulder was a scythe gleaming coldly in the light, mere inches from his neck.

"Garrr! (▼皿▼#)"

"Bertha, are you always this brave?" Even Steven couldn't help but lower his voice.

A woman's age and weight—two of the most dangerous topics—were things even he wouldn't dare touch.

Flint nodded knowingly. "It's fine, you get used to it. He won't die."

With that, he turned his gaze to the battlefield through the large floor-to-ceiling windows.

Under the deafening cheers of the crowd, Johan and his opponent for the day, Remo, stepped onto the battlefield.

Because of Remo's "provocative" actions the day before, the audience was especially hyped today.

Johan, as usual, remained composed, smiling and waving at familiar faces in the crowd.

On the other hand, Remo exuded confidence, greeting the audience with an air of self-assurance.

"So bold! I love it!"

"That's the spirit! Take down Johan-sensei, and we'll make you a general!"

"Finally, someone dares to challenge Johan-sensei again!"

Both trainers took their positions.

Remo, dressed in an outfit reminiscent of Orochimaru, with face paint and a paper umbrella in hand, looked like a fusion of Jiraiya and Orochimaru.

He arrogantly pointed his umbrella at Johan, his face full of confidence as he declared:

"Today, we will absolutely defeat at least one of your Pokémon!"

...??

The audience nearly tripped over themselves.

Sure, they were expecting some trash talk, but wasn't this a bit too extreme?

Their goal was simply to take down one of Johan-sensei's Pokémon?

But thinking about it...

It actually made sense.

After all, in this tournament, Johan had never actually let a single one of his Pokémon faint. He had only switched them out.

Johan smiled and nodded.

"Good luck."

Newer fans were touched by how kind Johan was, while veteran fans were already mourning for Remo.

They weren't afraid of Johan having a stern face—they were afraid of him smiling like that.

"Trainers, send out your Pokémon!" The referee signaled.

"Go!" Remo closed his umbrella and slung it over his shoulder while throwing out a Poké Ball. A burst of red light revealed his Pokémon.

Conkeldurr!

Two heavy concrete pillars slammed into the ground with a thud. Conkeldurr flexed its muscular arms, its gaze fierce and focused.

Remo had done his research on Johan—well, plenty of "experts" online had analyzed Johan's battle patterns.

They all came to one conclusion:

Johan-sensei had a "hidden compulsion"—he liked to send out Pokémon in a certain order, either from weakest to strongest or in the order he had caught them.

In his last battle, he had used Charcadet, a Pokémon that had never appeared on the battlefield before.

That meant today, he was likely to send out Kubfu.

Against a Fighting-type like Kubfu, Remo chose to go head-to-head with Conkeldurr to wear it down!

Under the watchful eyes of the audience, Johan released his first Pokémon.

A Pokémon with drooping head crests and a gentle smile hopped out of the Poké Ball, exuding warmth and friendliness.

Indeedee!

Big Sister!

"Ayeee~ (❁´◡`❁)"

Indeedee gracefully bowed to both Conkeldurr and Remo, as if greeting customers at a café.

For a moment, the entire stadium was stunned.

"Indeedee?!"

"So polite! AWSL!"

"Wait, isn't this one of the employees from Johan-sensei's café?!"

"Holy crap—this is straight-up mockery! He didn't even send out one of his main Pokémon; he just threw in a staff member!"

A...staff member?

Remo's expression stiffened.

Was Johan looking down on him that much?

He gripped his umbrella tightly.

Unforgivable!

But then, realization hit him.

If it was just an employee, didn't that mean he actually had a chance to defeat one of Johan-sensei's Pokémon?

And Indeedee was known for being a support Pokémon.

How much firepower could a support Pokémon possibly have?

This was his chance!

Hummmm—

The moment Indeedee appeared, a mystical energy spread from its body, covering the entire battlefield.

Psychic Surge activated! The battlefield was now a Psychic Terrain!

"Battle, begin!" The referee announced.

"Conkeldurr, use Hammer Arm!"

"Ka-kaah!!"

Conkeldurr gripped its concrete pillars and charged forward, each heavy footstep shaking the ground.

Johan's smile remained unchanged as he calmly commanded:

"Indeedee, Expanding Force."

"Ayeee~"

There was a hint of excitement in Indeedee's voice.

Finally! A real battle!

She was actually a bit thrilled.

Hummmm—

A surge of immense psychic power exploded from Indeedee, covering the battlefield in an instant.

As Conkeldurr charged, it suddenly felt an overwhelming force bearing down on it.

The sheer power crushed it within seconds.

"Ka-kaah!"

With a cry of agony, Conkeldurr collapsed to the ground with a loud thud.

For a brief moment, the entire stadium fell silent.

Even Remo stood frozen.

"Conkeldurr is unable to battle!" The referee declared.

"Ayeee~"

Indeedee politely bowed again, her eyes filled with innocence.

Huh?

I didn't even use that much power... how did it go down so fast?

The audience finally erupted into chaos.

"One-hit KO?! Conkeldurr just got obliterated?!"

"How can a Pokémon be this polite?"

"A polite one-hit KO?! That's too polite!"

Meanwhile, veteran fans who had seen Indeedee's battles online finally remembered—

This Indeedee was known as the seventh Battle Frontier facility boss Pokémon!

Remo took a deep breath, realizing his strategy had failed.

Without hesitation, he sent out his second Pokémon.

"Go, Bouffalant!"

A massive buffalo-like Pokémon with an enormous afro emerged.

Bouffalant, the Bash Buffalo Pokémon—a Normal-type known for having enough impact force to derail trains.

"Hey, Bertha, it's your relative!" Flint joked.

Bertha: "..."

Conkeldurr had been weak to Psychic, but Bouffalant had no such weakness and had high special defense.

"Use Head Charge!"

Bouffalant lowered its head, its afro glowing red as it charged full speed at Indeedee.

Its impact was far more terrifying than Conkeldurr's.

But Johan remained calm.

"Indeedee, don't worry. Expanding Force again."

"Ayeee~!"

A surge of Psychic energy flooded the battlefield once more, boosted by the Psychic Terrain.

BOOM!

The ground beneath Bouffalant shattered, forming a perfect square indentation.

Bouffalant collapsed instantly.

Silence.

Then—

"Another one-hit KO?!"

"This Indeedee is insanely strong!"

"And so, this is what happens when you provoke Johan-sensei… Smiling politely while destroying you!"

Remo swallowed hard.

Was it too late to apologize?

# 409: Instant Healing—The Paladin Indeedee! (BONUS)

"Smack!!"

Remo suddenly slapped himself.

Hiss—

Even Johan winced at the sight.

A bright red handprint instantly appeared on Remo's face, but his gaze was once again firm.

It wasn't over yet!

He was a proud and determined person—otherwise, he wouldn't have dared to provoke Johan in front of so many people. Just like how he would train relentlessly after losing to Alain in the future, he wasn't the type to give up just because he had already lost two Pokémon.

Taking a deep breath, he pulled out his final Poké Ball.

At this stage, there was no room for hesitation.

"Go, Chomp!!"

"Garrr—!!"

A burst of red light revealed a fierce Garchomp standing tall on the battlefield.

This was the foundation of Remo's confidence—the reason he dared to challenge Johan. His ace, the pseudo-legendary Garchomp!

"Garchomp, huh..." Flint instinctively shrank his neck and glanced at the Garchomp standing behind Cynthia with its arms crossed.

"Garrr! (▼皿▼#)"

He quickly averted his gaze.

Nope. Not the same at all.

"Johan-sensei's Indeedee seems to have gotten serious," Aaron noticed the change in its expression.

"Speaking of which, Johan-sensei's Indeedee is really strong."

As a specialist in Psychic-type Pokémon, Flint could feel the immense strength radiating from Indeedee.

"This Indeedee has never lost a battle in the café when facing challengers," Steven added.

"Never lost?"

Flint couldn't help but click his tongue. "Is this really just a 'café employee'?"

Steven chuckled. "It may seem that way, but... Sensei treats all his Pokémon the same. Whether it's food or items, Indeedee and Gengar receive the same treatment."

Indeedee, its younger sibling, and even Alcremie always got the highest-quality Pokéblocks, handmade by Johan himself.

Whenever Johan "restocked" on items like Dawn Stones, he never left them out either.

As a student of Johan and a regular at the café, Steven knew this well.

"Johan's sincere dedication to his Pokémon naturally earns him their unwavering loyalty," Steven mused.

"That's probably why his strength has grown so quickly."

Cynthia, who had been quietly listening, finally spoke, elegantly crossing her legs.

"Pokémon can sense the true hearts of their trainers and respond in kind—especially Pokémon like Indeedee, which are particularly attuned to emotions."

Indeedee had wanted to experience an official battle, so Johan had Steven bring it all the way from Hoenn.

Because Johan had promised it.

Of course, there was no denying that this Indeedee was exceptionally talented. But not everyone would train an Indeedee for battle.

The Battle Resumes!

"Chomp, show them the fruits of our training—Dragon Rush!" Remo shouted.

"Garrr—!!"

Garchomp swung its sharp crimson scythes and leaped high into the air, its body suddenly enveloped in the silhouette of a massive dragon.

"Indeedee, Trick Room."

Before Garchomp could complete its ascent, Johan and Indeedee moved.

"Ayeee~!"

Hummmm—

A transparent barrier expanded from Indeedee's center, covering the battlefield.

Speed reversal!

"Expanding Force!"

"Ayeee!!"

Rather than flinching at Garchomp's overwhelming presence, Indeedee's eyes sparkled with excitement.

A tidal wave of Psychic energy surged forth.

Remo knew all too well how devastating Expanding Force could be, but in this situation, there was no avoiding it.

Gritting his teeth, he gripped his umbrella tightly and swung it down, shouting:

"Chomp, charge through it!"

The vicious dragon pushed forward through the crushing weight of the Psychic energy, diving straight at Indeedee from above.

BOOM!!

Like a meteor slamming into the ground, the battlefield shook with a thunderous roar, sending up a massive cloud of dust.

"Did it hit? Is this a one-hit KO on Indeedee?"

"Not bad—Remo's Garchomp is actually pretty strong."

"Wait... is that... Heal Pulse?!"

As the dust settled, a soft green glow shimmered around Indeedee, restoring its condition back to a safe level.

Indeedee's eyes gleamed with Psychic energy, effortlessly holding Garchomp at bay just inches away.

It had been hit.

But Indeedee's Heal Pulse had instantly restored its health.

"Chomp!" Remo shouted again, his gaze sharp. "Outrage!"

"Garrr!!"

Garchomp's scythes scraped against Indeedee's Psychic barrier, producing an unsettling screech.

Its eyes turned blood-red as a violent aura erupted from its body, an ominous crimson energy swirling around it.

With a fierce roar, it swung its scythes down again.

CRACK!!

The Psychic barrier shattered as the attack sliced toward Indeedee.

"Ayeee!"

But Indeedee showed no fear.

One hand strengthened its Psychic defenses.

The other?

It simply patted itself with another Heal Pulse.

At that moment, the audience witnessed an unforgettable scene.

"Holy crap—is this instant healing?!"

"A healer tanking Garchomp's Outrage head-on?! This Indeedee is insane!"

"Hah! You're hitting me, but I'm healing faster than you can damage me!"

Even though Garchomp's attacks were fierce, they weren't enough to take down Indeedee.

And Indeedee, experienced as it was, handled the situation with remarkable ease.

Back when it had escaped with its younger sibling, this was exactly how it had fought—one hand healing, the other using Psychic power.

Indeedee's true specialty wasn't offense.

It was healing.

Just then, the Psychic Terrain faded.

"Chomp, now's our chance—go all out!!"

With the disappearance of the Psychic Terrain, Remo saw an opening.

Indeedee's advantage was gone—was it finally out of options?

Johan simply smiled and shook his head.

"Indeedee, let's finish this. Dazzling Gleam."

"Ayeee~!"

A blinding white light erupted from Indeedee's body.

"Garrr—!!"

Instinctively, Garchomp raised its arms to shield itself.

But within the overwhelming radiance, its body began to sizzle as if it were being scorched.

Garchomp let out a pained roar.

Then—

THUD!

Garchomp crashed onto the battlefield.

"Garchomp is unable to battle!" The referee waved the flag. "The winner is—Johan!"

"Ayeee~ (＾▽＾)"

Indeedee chirped happily. Upon realizing the battle was over, it immediately used Heal Pulse on Garchomp, soothing its injuries.

The audience erupted into another frenzy.

"So wholesome!"

"I heard Johan-sensei's Indeedee runs a café in the shopping district too!"

"Really?! Once the tournament's over, I'm buying a dozen pastries from there!"

Had this battle turned into a café advertisement?

After the Battle

Remo shook Johan's hand, his expression slightly ashamed.

"Thank you for the match. We lost."

"It's okay," Johan said with a smile, patting his shoulder. "You fought well too."

"Really?" Remo's eyes lit up with hope.

Johan smiled, blinked playfully, and said nothing.

Remo: "..."

SAY SOMETHING, DAMN IT!

Johan left the battlefield with Indeedee.

"Well? Was that satisfying?"

"Ayeee~"

Indeedee returned to its usual gentle demeanor, tilting its head playfully.

Johan chuckled, ruffling its fluffy head. "You little troublemaker."

Elsewhere—

"Garrr! (▼皿▼#)"

Cynthia's Garchomp crossed its arms and huffed out two puffs of white mist, clearly displeased.

That Garchomp... was way too weak.

It turned to Flint and gestured for him to follow.

"Garrr!"

"Let's spar."

Flint stiffened—then smirked.

"Fine! Let's go!"

Meanwhile—

As Johan and Indeedee walked down the hallway, a voice called out.

"Johan-sensei."

They turned to see a man politely placing a hand on his chest and bowing slightly.

Indeedee returned the gesture.

Johan raised an eyebrow.

"Tobias?"

Tobias had come looking for him.

"I'm honored that Johan-sensei knows of me."

As he spoke, Tobias's hand reached for his belt—

A duelist's instincts kicking in.
