# 410: The Rotom Who Dreams of Becoming the Strongest Photographer (BONUS)

Tobias was originally based on the 2009 Pokémon TCG World Champion in the anime.

To be honest—

Johan didn't have particularly strong feelings toward Tobias.

It wasn't because of his good looks—after all, no matter how handsome he was, he still couldn't compare to Johan or Steven.

It also wasn't because Tobias had both a Darkrai and a Latios—

After all, even a Officer Jenny had a Latias, and that was pretty normal.

The main reason?

It was probably because when Johan watched the anime years ago, Tobias had been a total 'deus ex machina.'

And not just any deus ex machina—he had zero buildup.

Everyone knew how it went—the "childhood friend" always lost to the surprise newcomer.

So naturally, most people weren't very fond of "out-of-nowhere" characters like that.

Cough.

Of course, Johan wasn't the type to let childhood frustrations from the anime influence his treatment of people in real life.

But—

Tobias was definitely worth paying attention to.

Originally, this was just supposed to be a fun and relaxing "Baby Cup."

But the moment a guy with Legendary Pokémon showed up, the entire tournament changed.

Then again…

Even Ash had nearly taken down two of Tobias's Legendaries.

So how strong could he really be?

Not to mention, this was before Ash had grown into his prime.

Tobias's actual strength was unknown.

Whether he even had Darkrai and Latios at this point was also unknown.

"Ka-kaah~~"

From the shadows, Gengar emerged.

Its crimson eyes gleamed menacingly, its signature cloak billowing dramatically.

It stared at Tobias's red cape, then at its own cape.

Hold on a second.

This guy… is copying my look?!

Tobias blinked at Gengar, especially at the matching cloak.

For a moment—

His mind went blank.

He and a Gengar… were wearing the exact same outfit?

Who would even believe that if he told them?

"Ahem."

Johan cleared his throat and gently pressed down on Gengar's head.

Then, he asked, "So, Tobias—what brings you here?"

Gengar, meanwhile, gave Tobias's shadow a subtle wink.

Tobias snapped out of his daze and smiled.

"I'm the founder of the Pokémon Trading Card Game."

"I'd like to discuss a business collaboration with you, Professor Johan."

"A collaboration?"

Wait—wasn't Matori the one in charge of these things?

But before Johan could say anything, Tobias continued—

"But we can talk about that later. For now… would you be interested in an exhilarating Pokémon TCG match?"

As he spoke, he pulled out a deck of cards—and even an extra deck, as if already prepared.

Johan's eyes narrowed.

This was… a duelist's challenge?

But before he could let his competitive side take over, Johan quickly shut it down and declined with a polite smile.

"Next time. Definitely next time."

Rule number one of life:

Never—under any circumstances—play cards against the person who MAKES the cards.

They can literally print new cards at will!

What if halfway through the game, Tobias suddenly whipped out a new rule and said—

"Johan-boy."

Tobias blinked.

…That was not the response he was expecting.

Seeing his confusion, Johan explained—

"The Lily of the Valley Conference is still ongoing. Right now, my focus is on the tournament, and to be honest… I'm not very good at card games."

Tobias paused, then nodded in understanding.

"That's fair. I was too forward."

Though he was slightly disappointed, he still smiled confidently and said—

"Then let us battle through our Pokémon instead, in this tournament!"

"Of course."

Johan nodded, secretly relieved that the topic had finally shifted back to normal.

"Then… I'll see you on the battlefield."

"Good!"

As Johan walked away, Tobias suddenly realized something.

Wait.

Wasn't I supposed to be discussing a business deal?

How did this turn into a Pokémon battle challenge?!

"Ah, well… it's all the same."

Tobias casually brushed back his hair.

"After all, our real goal is to use this tournament to promote Pokémon TCG."

"If I win the championship, it'll be great advertising."

Plus…

As a true card game enthusiast, Tobias had sensed something in Johan.

A kindred spirit.

Johan definitely understood the beauty of Pokémon TCG.

He was just too focused on the tournament right now.

But once the tournament was over—

There was no doubt in Tobias's mind.

He would absolutely get Johan to play a match with him!

From the shadows beneath Tobias's feet, Darkrai silently emerged.

"That Gengar was strong."

Darkrai's voice drifted through the air, a whisper as ethereal as mist.

Tobias smirked.

"Of course. We're up against Professor Johan, after all."

"You seem to respect him quite a bit," Darkrai observed.

"Of course. He's a teacher worthy of everyone's respect."

Darkrai nodded, recalling the moment Gengar had noticed it watching.

For some reason…

Darkrai had a very unsettling feeling.

That Gengar…

It might be far more terrifying than it seemed.

"Then let's go train," Darkrai said.

"If we want to win, we should at least prepare properly."

Tobias nodded in agreement.

"Mm. Just one more match, and then we'll train."

Darkrai: "…"

—

At the stadium entrance, Johan met up with Cynthia and the others.

"You're back already?" Flint grinned. "Who'd you run into?"

"Just a 'challenger,'" Johan said, pointing behind him.

"Oh?"

Flint's eyes lit up. "I'll go check them out!"

Without another word, he ran off toward where Johan had come from.

Cynthia, Fitzwilliam, Aime, and Johan simply stood there, unfazed.

Steven hesitated.

"Is that… okay?"

Johan waved dismissively. "It's fine."

Steven raised an eyebrow.

"Are you sure?"

Johan smiled.

"He's not gonna win."

The group froze.

Flint might be a bit reckless sometimes, but his strength was undeniable.

Aside from Cynthia, there weren't many people who could guarantee a win against him.

Especially when he got serious.

Seeing their confused expressions, Johan casually explained—

"It's a card game."

"Pfft—"

The entire group burst into laughter.

Right.

Flint might be a powerhouse in Pokémon battles—

But in card games?

Yeah.

He wasn't winning anything.

Meanwhile, at the stadium entrance—

Flint, holding a Poké Ball.

Tobias, holding a deck of cards.

Both men stared at each other in total confusion.

"Wait… aren't we battling?"

"Wait… aren't we playing cards?"

—

That evening, Johan's hotel room.

Knock, knock.

When Johan opened the door—

"Professor Oak?"

Johan blinked in surprise.

"What's with that look?" Professor Oak asked, amused.

Johan chuckled.

"Ahaha… I thought it was someone trying to hand me an 'underground business card.'"

Professor Oak looked even more confused.

"Underground business card?"

"Never mind. I just met a card game enthusiast earlier today."

"Oh, I see," Professor Oak nodded.

Though, in the back of his mind—

What do card games have to do with underground businesses?

Professor Oak stepped inside, pulling out a brand-new Pokédex from his bag.

"I've upgraded your Pokédex. This version has a more comprehensive database and is even better suited for Rotom."

He handed it over to Johan, who took it with curiosity.

Professor Oak briefly explained the new functions of the upgraded Pokédex.

"Go ahead and let Rotom try it out," he suggested.

"Rotom," Johan called out.

"Rotto~! Here I am!"

From somewhere deep in the room, Rotom shot out of an electrical socket, zipping through the air like a bolt of lightning.

The moment it saw the new Pokédex, its eyes lit up with excitement.

In an instant, it phased into the device.

Bzzt—!

The Pokédex's shape shifted slightly, adapting to Rotom's presence.

A face appeared on the screen, and small mechanical arms and legs extended from the device.

"Rotto~! The new Rotom Pokédex is ready to serve, rotto!"

Johan was momentarily stunned.

Wait a second…

Isn't this… the same design as the Alola Rotom Dex?!

Had they already developed this model so early?!

Professor Oak observed the changes with satisfaction.

"Looks like a perfect fit."

This upgraded Pokédex was specially designed to integrate seamlessly with Rotom. If everything worked well, Oak planned to mass-produce it for other Trainers.

Meanwhile—

From behind the doorframe, Gengar cautiously peeked out.

Its glowing red eyes darted toward the unexpected visitor.

The moment it saw Professor Oak, Gengar's entire body froze.

It had been curious about who had come knocking so late at night—

But the moment it saw who it was—

It immediately regretted it.

Too late.

Professor Oak had already noticed it.

"Oh? Gengar?"

"Ka-kaah!!"

For some reason—

The fearless, mischievous Gengar suddenly seemed nervous.

Johan was highly amused.

Gengar, who feared nothing and no one, was acting like a guilty child caught sneaking around.

Professor Oak chuckled warmly and walked toward it.

"I brought you a gift."

"Ka-kaah? (・・?)"

Johan ignored Gengar's panicked expression and turned back to Rotom.

"So, how do you like it?"

"Rotto~! It's amazing! I feel like I can store even more electricity now!"

"Want to take it out for a spin tomorrow?"

Rotom immediately sparked with excitement.

"Bzzzt! Really?! Rotto!!"

Johan smirked.

"Yeah. I've got a secret weapon."

Rotom's screen flashed brightly.

"A secret weapon?!"

Johan had only been joking, but Rotom took it completely seriously.

And not only that—

It seemed genuinely eager to take the stage.

Wait… is this thing starting to think like Gengar?!

"Why are you so hyped?" Johan raised an eyebrow.

Rotom's screen flickered as it explained,

"I'm just curious! If I take pictures from on stage instead of in the audience, will they turn out different, rotto?"

As a professional Pokémon photographer, Rotom never missed a chance to experiment with new angles and perspectives.

"Let's see how things go tomorrow," Johan said.

"Understood! Rotto!"

Truthfully, Rotom wasn't actually interested in battling.

It just wanted to take pictures from a new point of view.

If Johan had really meant for it to fight, or even sweep opponents one by one…

Yeah, that wasn't happening.

Compared to battling, Rotom still preferred photography.

"You'll get your chance," Johan said.

"Huh? Chance for what, rotto?"

Johan smirked.

"I'll make sure you get the chance to meet… the greatest Pokémon 'photographer' in the world."

Rotom's screen flared with excitement.

Johan coughed, suppressing the grin threatening to spread across his face.

Then, with a completely serious tone, he declared—

"The mission to take down the strongest 'photographer' is in your hands, Rotom!"

A Few Days Ago—

At a Pokémon Daycare, Johan had purchased an Egg.

But after taking it home, he realized—

It was fake.

A fake Pokémon Egg.

Could he let that slide?

Absolutely not.

He immediately reported it to Officer Jenny.

And today—

She had contacted him again, saying she needed more details about the fake Pokémon Egg case.

So, Johan had gone in to give his statement and cooperate with the investigation.

Fortunately, he had a good relationship with Officer Jenny.

Together, they had managed to track down the seller's contact information.

When they called…

They discovered—

The seller was actually a Pokémon Hunter.

And so—

Cough.

# 411: Card Battle with Charcadet? (BONUS)

January 20th.

Johan woke up to the sound of rhythmic clanging.

When he got out of bed, he was surprised to find that even Ninetales wasn't curled up under the covers—it had already gotten up.

Huh?

Did the sun rise from the west today?

The little one actually didn't stay in bed.

Then, as he scanned the room, he realized that none of his Pokémon were there.

He got out of bed, followed the sound of clanking, and slid open the door to the garden—sure enough, all his Pokémon were there, gathered around Tinkaton.

Tinkaton had a serious expression, hammering away in a steady rhythm, sparks flying with each strike.

After a while, it placed a glowing red piece of metal into Charcadet's hands, using its heat to keep the material from cooling down.

Dragonite occasionally flapped its wings to help Charcadet maintain the temperature.

Was this...

A minimalist version of Ornn's "Living Forge"?

"Cha!"

Tinkaton let out a short cry, and Kubfu quickly used its fluffy arms to wipe the sweat from Tinkaton's forehead.

Kubfu had turned into an assistant now?

Before long, Tinkaton put down its hammer and called out to Ninetales.

"Oowoo~"

Without hesitation, Ninetales exhaled a chilling breath. With a sizzling sound and a rising white mist, the object Tinkaton had forged gradually took shape.

Johan suddenly understood.

A Ninetales-brand quenching agent!

Where was Gengar?

"Ka-kaah~ Ka-kaah~"

Gengar, wearing a pair of sunglasses, was floating nearby, overseeing the whole process.

Oh—

Not supervising Tinkaton and the others, but directing Rotom to record the entire forging process so that Tinkaton could review it later.

As for Larvitar...

"Yoji! (ˊ〇ˋ)"*

It had its mouth wide open, trying to catch the sparks flying off Tinkaton's hammer.

Of course, it couldn't catch them all with just its mouth, so its personal "bedpan" was placed nearby to collect the rest—double coverage.

No waste allowed! Larvitar was setting an example!

What a display of frugality.

So...

Had this whole group actually formed a complete forging assembly line?

Once Tinkaton finished forging two objects, it was time for the crucial fitting test.

"Cha!!"

Tinkaton solemnly handed the items to Charcadet.

"Goka!"

Charcadet was just as serious, receiving the objects and equipping them.

A golden cannon and a dark purple short sword!

One representing the path of Armarouge, the other representing Ceruledge.

And honestly?

Now that Charcadet was wearing them, it actually looked pretty cool.

"Goka!"

Charcadet gave a serious swing of its dark purple sword, then raised its arm as if aiming the cannon.

Johan's expression turned strange.

So they'd been working all morning on… this?

It seemed like nothing more than decoration—

But before that thought could fully form, Charcadet's eyes suddenly sharpened, and it lightly swung the sword.

Hiss!!

A crimson flame ignited along the blade, quickly shifting to a ghostly purple. As Charcadet swung, a faint purple energy trail was left in the air.

Johan's eyes widened.

The Bitter Blade?!

Bitter Blade: A Fire-type move, Power 90. Focuses lingering regrets at the tip of the sword and strikes the opponent. Heals for half the damage dealt.

This was Ceruledge's signature move—

And the only Fire-type move that could restore HP by attacking!

Granted, Charcadet's swing wasn't nearly at the level of a real Bitter Blade, and the [Pokédex] didn't even recognize it as the move yet.

But there was no denying that the form was starting to take shape.

"Goka!"

Then, flames flared up around Charcadet's body as it raised the golden cannon.

The fire gathered into the barrel, and within the flames, a strand of golden Life's Fire seemed to resonate with the cannon.

At the same time, the fire inside pulsed with Aura Energy.

The power behind this attack...

"So, is this an Armor Cannon? Or one enhanced with Aura?"

Armor Cannon: A Fire-type move, Power 120. Burns away part of its own armor to fire a powerful shot, lowering its Defense and Special Defense.

Johan was stunned.

He hadn't expected Tinkaton's study of the forging scrolls Armor of Celebration and Cursed Armor to actually produce something useful.

Was this an early test to see which armor would suit Charcadet best?

Or—

Was Tinkaton planning to forge both armors?

Like a card battle transformation, swapping between them?

Johan's expression turned even stranger. That would be pretty crazy.

"Cha."

Tinkaton, having witnessed the entire process, rubbed its chin in thought.

This forging session had deepened its understanding of Charcadet's connection with the two armors.

Even Johan had to admit—Tinkaton's craftsmanship was impressive.

Truly professional.

At the same time, a wild idea popped into Johan's mind.

If... if Tinkaton could learn how to forge swords and shields,

Could it replicate them?

A Pokémon World version of Cloudsmith?

Meanwhile, across the region...

Gordie, dressed in a brown jacket and stylish sunglasses, had a determined look. Underneath, he wore Galar's standard Trainer uniform, with the number 188—symbolizing "Rock Wall."

He was one of Galar's rising stars, along with Bea and Nessa, viewed as a future Gym Leader.

His mother was Melony, Circhester's Ice-type Gym Leader.

But while he received elite training from her, their philosophies differed. Instead of Ice-types, Gordie followed his true passion—Rock-types.

He wanted to redefine the image of Rock Pokémon, proving they weren't just brute force.

To achieve this, he left home, challenging Trainers far and wide, earning both victories and a devoted fanbase—

A fanbase mostly composed of women.

To show appreciation for his supporters, Gordie had even released two photo books!

Cough.

This was his first time leaving Galar to challenge another region's League. He wanted to study how their tournaments differed from Galar's.

And so far, he had proven himself in battle—while also charming even more fans.

Now...

When he learned his next opponent was the legendary Professor Johan, Gordie realized—

This might be his strongest opponent yet!

So, after a moment of hesitation, he called Leon.

"Leon, sir."

Gordie sat in front of his computer, watching Johan's battle footage.

"Any advice for facing Professor Johan?"

Leon, currently lost while looking for a flight path, was surprised.

"Your next opponent is Johan?"

"Yeah, I just got the news."

Gordie knew that Leon had battled Johan before. He also knew that Johan had defeated Mustard, Galar's former Champion.

That fact alone carried massive weight.

Leon thought for a moment, then said:

"If you're fighting Professor Johan... be especially careful of his Eevee."

"...Eevee?"

Gordie was baffled.

He had watched tons of Johan's battles—

What Eevee?!

"Besides Eevee, also his Gengar, Ninetales, Tinkaton, Dragonite, Kubfu, Charcadet—"

Gordie: "..."

Gordie felt like he had asked the wrong person.

Who didn't know Johan's entire team?!

Leon coughed.

"Alright, but seriously—his Kubfu is the most talented one my mentor has ever seen."

Hearing that, Gordie's expression grew serious.

"Got it. I'll be ready."

After a pause, he added, "Thanks, Leon."

He was about to hang up when—

"Wait! Gordie, do you know how to fly to Ballonlea?"

Gordie: "..."

"Ladies and gentlemen! Welcome to the Lily of the Valley Conference's Top 8 Qualifiers!"

The announcer's voice echoed throughout the massive main stadium, igniting the crowd's excitement.

At this stage of the tournament, all matches moved from the secondary arenas to the main stadium.

However, the format remained 3v3 rotation battles.

Only when competitors reached the Top 8, advancing to the quarterfinals, would the format change to 6v6 full battles.

"Today's challenger is Gordie from the Galar region! Here's his profile."

In the private viewing box, Fitzwilliam handed Johan a file on Gordie.

Johan took it, briefly flipping through.

Honestly, when he learned yesterday that Gordie would be his opponent, he didn't really need the report.

He already knew plenty about Gordie.

After all, this was the future Rock-type Gym Leader of Circhester.

Seeing Johan put the file away, Cynthia asked,

"So… have you decided which tricky strategy you're going with?"

Wait—

Did she almost say dirty strategy just now?!

Hmph.

Johan smirked.

"More or less."

Meanwhile, in another part of the stadium, Officer Jenny and I had uncovered new information.

After speaking to Jenny, we had confirmed that the suspect was a Pokémon Hunter—

But he refused to admit it.

Not only did he deny his identity, but he also insisted that the Pokémon Egg he sold me was real,

Even going so far as to falsely accuse me of fraud.

I was furious.

The problem was…

I had no solid proof that the egg was fake—

All I had was a transaction record.

But soon, we found a breakthrough.

We managed to contact another victim!

And it turned out…

That victim had been scammed for twice the amount I had lost!

At last, the case seemed to be making progress.

# 412: The Toxic Ladder Strategy, Tactical Rotom!

As the announcer introduced the match, it was time for Johan and Gordie's battle.

Amid the wave of cheers from the crowd, both trainers stepped onto the battlefield.

Judging from the audience's reaction, both of them had a solid fanbase.

"Professor Johan, please take care of me!" Gordie was unexpectedly polite.

"Likewise," Johan responded with a smile.

"Trainers, send out your Pokémon!" The referee raised the flag.

At the same time, Johan and Gordie each sent out their first Pokémon.

"Go, Barbaracle!"

Gordie's first Pokémon was Barbaracle, the Rock- and Water-type Pokémon known as the Collective Pokémon!

Its appearance was quite peculiar—at first glance, it looked like seven barnacle-like limbs fixed onto two large rocks, forming its body. Even its "head" was just another barnacle limb.

And Johan's first Pokémon was… Rotom!

"Barbaracle, the Collective Pokémon! A Rock- and Water-type. It is said that each of its limbs can move independently, Rotom!"

The moment Rotom appeared, it didn't forget its "primary job."

"Wait, is that… a Pokédex?!"

"Hold on, did Professor Johan just send out a Pokédex?"

"But that Pokédex… looks like a real Pokémon…"

"…"

The crowd was in an uproar when they saw the Pokédex-shaped Rotom on the field. Even Gordie looked a bit taken aback.

He had watched a lot of Johan's battle footage, but… he had never found the Eevee that Leon mentioned, nor had he ever seen this Rotom before.

However, Gordie didn't think Johan was sending out Rotom to mock him. Instead, he felt…

This might be another one of Johan's hidden aces.

After all, that legendary super strong Eevee Leon mentioned—almost no one had actually seen it. It was rumored to be one of Johan's strongest secret weapons.

If that was the case, this Rotom probably wasn't weak either.

Not to mention, as an Electric- and Ghost-type, Rotom had a strong advantage against Barbaracle.

"Barbaracle, stay alert. Take this seriously!"

"Skaaah!!"

Barbaracle took a battle stance, ready to fight.

"Rotom, Roto! Σ(дlll)"

But Rotom?

Rotom was just a "small, weak, and helpless" photographer! It didn't need this kind of intensity…

While frantically taking pictures, Rotom spun in circles toward the audience stands.

It could clearly feel the difference between filming on the field versus off the field—the opponent, the crowd, the big screen, the torches… everything was different.

And with so many people and so many cameras watching…

It was a bit nervous.

"Rotom, you done taking pictures? Switch forms."

From Johan's shadow, Gengar emerged—then opened its mouth wide and spat out a… washing machine!

"O-On it, Roto!"

Rotom quickly snapped a few more photos, then zipped back to Johan's side before leaving the Pokédex and diving into the washing machine.

The moment it entered, the washing machine's form changed, taking on Rotom's signature markings.

The Toxic Ladder Strategy. The Root of All Evil. The Infamous Water-Washing Machine. Wash Rotom!

Rotom still wasn't that common at this point, so many spectators were seeing it for the first time—especially its Washer Form.

"It transformed?!"

"First it was a Pokédex, and now it's a washing machine?"

"What kind of Pokémon is Rotom?!"

Wash Rotom, when fused with a washing machine, changed its typing to Electric/Water.

This unique typing, combined with its Levitate ability, meant that its only weakness was Grass-type moves.

Gordie frowned slightly.

He knew about Wash Rotom, but this was his first time battling against one. He wasn't entirely sure how to deal with it.

Johan patted Rotom, who was still a little nervous, and said:

"I've given you a secret weapon. Don't worry—if you don't want to fight, just run."

"R-Roto…" (⊙﹏⊙)ゞ

Rotom nodded vigorously, feeling relieved.

No problem. If things got rough, it could just run away—after all, it had already taken enough pictures!

"Battle, begin!"

With both trainers ready, the referee officially started the match.

As soon as the flag dropped, Gordie made the first move.

"Barbaracle, Shell Smash!"

Shell Smash: Shatters its own shell, lowering Defense and Special Defense while significantly increasing Attack, Special Attack, and Speed.

This was a classic all-out offense strategy for Barbaracle—trading defense for maximum power.

Crack!!

A visible crack appeared on Barbaracle's rocky shell, and in return, both its offensive stats and Speed skyrocketed. Its aura surged.

Feeling Barbaracle's intimidating presence, Rotom tensed up.

But after going through so many crazy experiences with Johan—even facing Regigigas and Heatran—it managed to stay calm.

So, as soon as Gordie shouted his command, and Johan responded, Wash Rotom made its move.

"Rotom, Trick."

Trick: Swaps held items with the target, leaving both Pokémon locked into their new items.

Whoooom—

A blue light surrounded Rotom, enveloping both it and Barbaracle. Then, their held items swapped places.

Rotom received Barbaracle's Focus Sash.

And Barbaracle? It received Rotom's held item… a Choice Scarf!

"Wait… what?! Trick into Choice Scarf?!"

"Holy crap, Johan, have some mercy!"

"Barbaracle can only keep using Shell Smash now?! Endless Shell Smash Barbaracle?!"

Some spectators had assumed that Johan had changed his battle style after his past few aggressive matches.

But now, today, right in front of them—

He was back to his usual dirty tricks.

Even Gordie's eyes widened.

Wait.

Being locked into Shell Smash?!

How was he supposed to play like this?!

Honestly? Gordie had no idea how to handle this situation.

"Rotom, Thunderbolt."

A Trick-Choice Scarf Wash Rotom—that was textbook toxicity.

"Roto!"

Seeing that its opponent was stuck in an infinite Shell Smash loop, Rotom immediately relaxed. It unleashed a burst of dazzling golden electricity, striking Barbaracle head-on.

Boom!!

"Return, Barbaracle!"

"Skaaaah!" Barbaracle staggered back from the electric attack, just as it was recalled into its Poké Ball.

Gordie frowned.

As expected of Johan's Pokémon—his reaction time was insanely fast. Even though he had managed to withdraw Barbaracle in time, it still took some damage.

At least…

It wasn't knocked out.

Actually, Johan was internally sighing.

Withdrawing a Pokémon mid-attack—this was one of the annoying loopholes in real-world Pokémon battles.

Using a recall to dodge damage.

However, since Gordie had withdrawn Barbaracle and was summoning a new Pokémon, Johan and Rotom had time to act.

"Roto!"

Rotom was already charged up and ready to go.

Gordie took a deep breath and threw out his second Poké Ball.

"Your turn, Stonjourner!"

Emerging onto the battlefield was a massive Pokémon composed of large, irregularly shaped rocks. Its legs were especially thick and sturdy, making it well-suited for powerful kicking attacks.

Bzzzt—

As Stonjourner appeared, golden static sparks burst from Rotom, striking the Rock-type Pokémon right on entry.

Thunder Wave!

Stonjourner was paralyzed!

Johan was a little surprised by Gordie's choice.

He had originally thought Gordie would send out his Contrary Shuckle, using its Fortress Strategy to stall.

But Stonjourner…

That was an interesting pick.

Because Stonjourner had only one ability: Power Spot.

Power Spot: Increases the power of allied Pokémon's attacks by 30%.

This ability made Stonjourner a great support Pokémon in doubles.

But in single battles?

Even as Johan processed this, Gordie remained confident. He clenched his fist and called out:

"Stonjourner, Wonder Room!"

Whooom—

Stonjourner lifted both arms, and an eerie, shimmering barrier descended onto the battlefield.

Within this Wonder Room, the Defense and Special Defense stats of all Pokémon swapped!

"Oh, I get it now! Stonjourner has insane physical Defense but terrible Special Defense. With Wonder Room, its Special Defense just skyrocketed!"

"And since Stonjourner is naturally super slow, the paralysis Speed drop doesn't really matter."

"Rotom is a Special Attacker, right?"

"But… Stonjourner is already paralyzed, and knowing Professor Johan…"

Johan had anticipated this move, so he immediately followed up:

"Rotom, Will-O-Wisp."

"Roto!"

A burst of blue spectral flames ignited around Stonjourner, burning it on the spot.

Stonjourner is now burned!

The audience instantly knew what was coming.

They had seen this before.

A few days ago, Johan had played the role of an all-out attacker.

Today? He was back to his status-inflicting, stall-heavy, toxic playstyle!

"Stonjourner, Gravity!" Gordie called out, staying unfazed.

Bzzzt—

Arcs of energy crackled around Stonjourner as it released a strange, invisible force.

Suddenly, a distorted gravity field spread across the battlefield.

Stonjourner's massive stone legs sank slightly, and dark energy rippled outward from its core.

Wash Rotom's Levitate ability was nullified—forcing it to land on the ground!

"Roto!!"

But Rotom was unfazed. The washing machine's front door swung open, and a surge of water burst out, rushing straight toward Stonjourner!

Hydro Pump!

The attack hit dead on!

Under the effects of Gravity, all moves except one-hit KO attacks had increased accuracy.

And Hydro Pump—one of Wash Rotom's strongest Water-type moves—was no exception.

However, because of Wonder Room, Stonjourner's Special Defense was now swapped with its high physical Defense.

So, despite taking a super-effective hit…

It survived!

"Rotom, again—Hydro Pump!"

Another powerful torrent of water shot out, slamming into Stonjourner!

"Stonjourner, Earthquake!"

Bzzzt—

But this time, the paralysis kicked in—and Stonjourner failed to move!

If Gordie had gotten just one successful Earthquake off, Rotom might've been in trouble.

But… paralysis had finally worked in Johan's favor.

And with the burn damage ticking away, Stonjourner collapsed.

Stonjourner is unable to battle!

Gordie… smiled.

The Stone Formation was now complete.

Now, the real battle was about to begin!

# 413: Do You Understand the Value of a ‘Tactical Master’?

"You did well, Stonjourner."

Gordie spoke to the Poké Ball as he recalled his Pokémon, then immediately took out his third Poké Ball.

Without any hesitation, he tossed it forward.

His confidence remained unwavering—if anything, he looked even more confident than before.

As if the previous battle hadn't affected him at all.

"Go, Coalossal!"

This was Gordie's signature Pokémon.

It resembled a massive, bipedal dinosaur, its entire body composed of hardened, jet-black stone. On its back, it carried a coal mound, glowing with a deep red heat, constantly emitting waves of white-hot steam.

Coalossal, the Coal Pokémon, was a Rock/Fire-type.

But truthfully, this wasn't the most ideal typing—it had double weaknesses to both Ground and Water and an additional weakness to Rock.

However, if used correctly…

"The Stonjourner left behind its stones on the field!"

At that moment, the announcer pointed out something most of the audience had overlooked.

As Coalossal entered the field, the scattered Stonjourner's Rocks from earlier began to glow faintly.

"Wait… is he activating Power Spot like this?!"

"Holy crap—so this is how you trigger Power Spot in singles?!"

"Learned it! Learned it!"

The crowd erupted in shock.

Clearly, this was Gordie's exclusive strategy—something most trainers wouldn't even think of. Even if they tried to mimic it, they'd likely fail.

Johan, who had already noticed this earlier, twitched the corner of his mouth.

"Yeah. Figures."

So we're just outright breaking the game now, huh?

This was basically a 2v1.

The Stonjourner's Rocks remained on the field, activating Power Spot, massively boosting Coalossal's attack power.

And yet… Gordie wasn't done.

Adjusting his glasses, he confidently waved his hand.

"Coalossal—Steam Engine, activate!"

"Garrr!!"

The coal on Coalossal's back glowed an intense crimson, and thick white steam gushed out like a raging furnace.

Under this steam boost, Coalossal's entire body became blindingly fast!

Its Steam Engine ability had triggered!

Steam Engine: When hit by a Water- or Fire-type attack, Speed is maximized!

Maximized.

That meant it instantly jumped up by SIX stages!

"Wait—Coalossal hasn't even been attacked yet?!"

"Yeah! How did Steam Engine activate?!"

The audience was confused.

"It's the water!"

But the sharp-eyed announcer noticed something most had missed.

The lingering water from Rotom's Hydro Pumps earlier—Coalossal had absorbed it!

Coalossal had landed right inside a crater left by Stonjourner's Earthquake.

That crater was flooded with leftover water from Rotom's repeated attacks.

And now, that water was visibly evaporating—turning into the steam Coalossal was expelling from its back.

"Wait—he can do that?!"

"Alright. I've officially learned something new today."

"Professor Johan is in danger!"

The audience was stunned by Gordie's strategy.

From the very moment he sent out Stonjourner, he had been setting up for Coalossal's entrance.

No—

Even Barbaracle was part of the setup!

He probably intended to get Barbaracle to use a Water-type move on Coalossal… but Johan's Choice Scarf Trick strategy had disrupted his plan.

In single battles, it was rare but not unheard of for two Pokémon to be sacrificed just to set up for a sweeper.

And now—

Coalossal had successfully powered up.

Bzzzt—

Johan sighed at Gordie's setup but wasn't about to sit around and watch.

He wasn't some gentleman who'd let his opponent finish buffing up before attacking.

So, the moment Coalossal entered the field…

A Thunder Wave was inevitable.

Since Coalossal couldn't be burned as a Fire-type, Paralysis was the next best thing.

"Garrr!!"

Hit by the electric current, Coalossal glared at Rotom, its eyes burning red-hot.

"R-Roto… (⊙﹏⊙)ゞ"

Rotom was freaking out.

I'M JUST A CAMERA BOT, MAN!!!

"Relax," Johan chuckled.

"R-Roto…"

He was still smiling at a time like this?! Rotom didn't even dare to meet Coalossal's eyes.

But even so—

Johan's calm and confidence soothed it.

At the very least…

Rotom trusted Johan wouldn't just throw it away.

"Now, it's our turn. Coalossal—Earthquake!"

"Garrr!!"

Coalossal roared and stomped the ground with full force. The entire battlefield rumbled, sending shockwaves racing toward Rotom.

Because the Gravity effect from Stonjourner was still active, Earthquake was the best possible move to use.

"Protect," Johan ordered calmly.

"Roto!"

As the ground split apart, Rotom instantly projected a green energy barrier, blocking the full force of the Earthquake.

Seeing his attack stopped, Gordie wasn't surprised—he simply pressed forward.

"Coalossal—Earthquake again!"

"Substitute."

With a flash, Rotom created a dummy, taking the hit instead.

"Wait, what? Protect—Substitute—Protect?!"

"Is Professor Johan just stalling for time?"

"Didn't that just waste his Focus Sash?"

"Wait, is he trying to wait out Coalossal's Paralysis?"

"Hah! Professor Johan doesn't have luck—he should know that! Third Earthquake incoming!"

The audience laughed as the third Earthquake closed in.

But at this moment—Johan's expression turned serious.

"Drop the Focus Sash. Run to the edge of the field!"

"Roto!"

Rotom immediately threw away its Focus Sash and rushed toward the edge of the battlefield, with Earthquake chasing right behind.

However—

Coalossal's Earthquake was much faster.

"What's he doing?! Dropping weight to run faster?"

"That won't work! Earthquake will still hit!"

"Professor Johan is panicking!"

Johan narrowed his eyes, ignoring the noise.

Even though Rotom was panicked, it still trusted Johan's judgment—even as the battlefield beneath it started cracking.

"Now! Trick!"

Whooom—

Rotom swapped its nonexistent Focus Sash with Coalossal's held item.

A faint clicking noise rang out.

If the announcer had been more experienced, they would have gasped—Rotom had just obtained Coalossal's Weakness Policy!

Weakness Policy: When hit by a super-effective attack, Attack and Special Attack sharply increase!

Rotom's power skyrocketed.

"Roto!"

POWER!!

At the same instant—

"Fly up!"

The moment Johan's voice fell, Gravity disappeared.

Rotom lifted off the ground—

Avoiding most of the Earthquake's damage!

Levitate was back!

And then—

"Hydro Pump!"

WHOOSH—

A raging torrent of water gushed from Rotom's washing machine door, engulfing Coalossal.

Gordie's expression completely changed.

"Coalossal—Protect!"

A green energy shield emerged—blocking the first Hydro Pump.

But—

Johan knew Coalossal would try to Protect.

That's why he waited to get Weakness Policy before attacking.

Crack—

The barrier shattered.

Rotom's second Hydro Pump blasted through.

BOOM!!

Coalossal collapsed.

Silence.

The stadium went completely quiet.

Some spectators had witnessed everything—

But their brains hadn't processed it yet.

The VIP room remained silent for a moment.

Then—

"Brilliant!"

Thorne couldn't help but exclaim in admiration.

Most of the spectators had no idea what had just happened, but Thorne, Cynthia, Steven, and the others understood everything perfectly.

"Professor Johan calculated the exact moment Gravity would end," Steven said, shaking his head in amazement. "He timed everything perfectly—just to steal Coalossal's Weakness Policy."

"That's also why he had Rotom run toward the edge of the battlefield and delay taking Earthquake damage," Cynthia added. "If the timing was even slightly off, Rotom might have been instantly KO'd."

"But if he did it too early, Weakness Policy wouldn't have activated."

"The only way to maximize his advantage…"

"…was to trigger Weakness Policy at the exact moment Gravity disappeared—so Rotom could Levitate again and dodge the next attack."

Thorne smacked his lips.

"Man… you call this guy a 'tactical master', but this isn't tactics anymore. This is freakin' wizardry."

The group chuckled.

It was true.

This wasn't just about tactics.

It was Johan's ability to predict, adapt, and execute flawlessly—

Even in a split-second window.

"To call him 'lucky' would be an insult," Steven said.

"He wasn't lucky," Cynthia agreed. "He just played it perfectly."

But then—

Flint raised his hand.

"I got a question."

Everyone turned to look at him.

"How did Professor Johan know Coalossal was holding a Weakness Policy?"

That was a fair question.

Because if Johan had misread Coalossal's held item, this entire plan would have backfired.

Thorne glanced at Flint and smirked.

"A 'tactical master' doesn't need to guess," he said smugly.

Flint: "…"

Johan, if he were here, would have rolled his eyes.

Steam Engine Coalossal carrying Weakness Policy?

That wasn't even a question.

That was basic knowledge.

Of course, he also had the Pokédex scan data as a backup.

"But…" Riley suddenly spoke, his tone thoughtful. "If Professor Johan had just a little bit more luck… maybe Coalossal would have been fully paralyzed earlier, and it wouldn't have been such a close fight."

Everyone paused.

Then, all at once—

They burst into laughter.

Johan? Lucky?

Come on.

Johan had never won a battle of pure luck in his life.

"A brilliant tactical display!"

By now, the announcer had finally caught up with what happened.

After consulting with several experts, he quickly explained the battle's key moments to the audience.

As the explanation spread, the crowd's confusion turned into shock… then awe.

Then—

Excitement.

"Wait, so that's why he threw away the Focus Sash—so Coalossal wouldn't get it!"

"This strategy is insane! If you misread the opponent's held item, you could completely screw yourself over!"

"You guys don't get it. Even if I knew my opponent's item, I still wouldn't be able to pull this off!"

"Not only did Professor Johan predict everything, but he also timed everything down to the second! That's what makes him a tactical master!"

"Okay, now I understand why people call him that…"

"Wait, so what would've happened if Hydro Pump missed at the very end?"

Everyone fell silent.

Then, someone muttered—

"…Professor Johan must've prayed to Arceus."

"Gordie, are you ready for your next Pokémon?"

The referee's voice brought Gordie back to reality.

He took a deep breath.

He had to admit—

He lost fair and square.

His strategy had been strong.

But Johan's improvisation had been even stronger.

Leon was right.

Battling Johan was an opportunity to learn.

He had just been given a real lesson in tactics.

His last Pokémon was still Barbaracle.

But against a double-boosted Wash Rotom…

There was no hope.

A few moments later—

"Barbaracle is unable to battle!"

"The winner is Professor Johan!"

"R-Roto…!!"

As soon as the battle ended, Rotom immediately zipped out of the washing machine, shaking uncontrollably.

It still couldn't believe it.

It just 1v3'd someone.

It was just a CAMERA BOT.

AND IT JUST SWEPT A TEAM.

It wasn't supposed to be strong!

It was supposed to just take pictures!!

Johan walked over and patted its head.

"Good job," he said with a grin.

Rotom twitched.

It had never been this stressed in its life.

"Professor Johan."

Gordie walked over, extending his hand.

"Thank you," he said sincerely. "That was an incredible battle. I learned a lot."

Johan shook his hand with a smile.

"Just luck," he said casually.

Gordie gave him a weird look.

Luck?

Bro.

Johan was literally the most unlucky trainer Gordie had ever seen.

If anyone had good luck that match, it was him—Coalossal didn't get paralyzed a single time despite the Thunder Wave.

Johan had zero luck.

What he had…

Was insane tactics.

But Johan just shrugged.

Deep down, though—

He was genuinely relieved.

Because if that last Hydro Pump had missed…

All his strategies would've gone straight into the garbage.

80% accuracy landing at the clutch moment?

That's not tactics.

That's Arceus showing mercy.

# 414: Is It Really Just Professor Johan’s Luck?

Fixed chapters 411 and 412. Reread them if you want, nothing much changes.

-----------

After the match.

Cynthia and the others found Johan in the tournament's "junkyard."

Ahem.

To be more precise, it was the discarded battlefield debris storage area.

"Yoji~(^^)"

Larvitar was happily gnawing on a large chunk of stone, the sound of crunching echoing in the air. Anyone unaware might've thought it was munching on potato chips.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar stood proudly beside it, arms crossed in satisfaction.

It was the one who found this place.

And the stone Larvitar was chewing on?

It was a leftover chunk from Stonjourner's battle earlier.

The reason Stonjourner's Power Spot ability had activated was because of the energy stored inside these stones.

For Larvitar, that was a massive temptation.

If not for being in the middle of a battle earlier, Larvitar probably would've charged in to steal them on the spot.

"This… is next-level frugality," Flint muttered as he watched Larvitar sitting in a pile of rubble.

Maybe this was the difference between him and Professor Johan?

They were both "poor," but Professor Johan even scavenged in junkyards.

Maybe he needed to learn from this?

After all, thriftiness is a virtue.

Infernape: You wanna hear yourself right now?!

"If you need minerals, Professor Johan… should I have a plane deliver some over?" Steven offered.

Johan: "…"

…Are we using entire planes as a unit of measurement now?!

He waved it off.

"No need. I'll restock once we get back—Larvitar is just curious about the taste."

Johan only wanted to spend a little—Steven just opened up an entire whale-sized transaction.

If a plane-full of minerals arrived, how was he supposed to transport them all?!

Even with Gengar's dimensional storage, it'd take forever to pack up.

Still, he did plan to buy a batch later—education was important, even if they were broke.

Once the tournament was over and he claimed the prize money, he'd ship some supplies home.

"Fair enough," Steven nodded, understanding the logistical issue.

Cynthia, however, looked thoughtful.

Seems like I should keep an eye out for rare stones next time I explore ancient ruins…

The Top 32 to Top 16 battles had concluded.

Throughout the day, there had been many thrilling matches.

But the most talked-about match was, without a doubt—

Johan vs. Gordie.

Many fans called it the most exciting battle of the entire Lily of the Valley Conference so far.

Both trainers showcased strategies that defied conventional understanding, pushing the match to an extremely high level.

Many believed that Gordie was strong enough to go further in the tournament, but unfortunately, he encountered Professor Johan and was eliminated.

Despite his loss, Gordie still received widespread praise.

As for Johan, his victory only further cemented his reputation, making him a major topic of discussion that evening.

Online discussions were equally heated.

"I never thought I'd see the day… where a 'Pokédex' would go on a 1v3 rampage."

"Bro, I just checked the stats. 'Wash Rotom' is basically a bugged Pokémon!"

"That's not the point! The real bug here is that Professor Johan is the one using it!"

"I seriously think Johan has a bunch of hidden strategies he hasn't even used yet."

"+1"

"+1"

"+1—Wait, STOP CHAINING +1s!"

Of course, people weren't just talking about Johan's match—

Another hot topic was Tobias.

Since the start of the tournament, Tobias had only ever used one Pokémon.

And he only needed one.

Darkrai.

If his opponent was weak, Darkrai just steamrolled them outright.

If his opponent was stronger, Darkrai used Dark Void + Dream Eater to shut them down.

So far—

Tobias had yet to reveal a second Pokémon.

Such a dominant performance inevitably led to comparisons with Johan.

Back in the Silver Conference, Johan had done something similar—

Except, instead of sweeping with one Pokémon, he rotated his entire team.

Some people argued that Johan's approach showed more depth, while others claimed that Tobias's unknown roster made him even more terrifying.

Either way—

Everyone agreed that this Lily of the Valley Conference was truly stacked with competition.

Sitting in a café, Johan scrolled through the online discussions with mild curiosity.

He, too, was curious about Tobias's other Pokémon.

At that moment—

"The matchups for tomorrow are out," Thorne announced, checking his tablet.

"Oh? Who's he up against?"

Flint immediately leaned in.

Thorne's expression turned serious.

"Kabu."

Kabu?!

The former Hoenn trainer who later became a Gym Leader in Galar—

And who had now been temporarily dismissed due to certain reasons.

Johan twitched slightly and turned to Cynthia.

"Does the Sinnoh League have a grudge against me or something?"

It felt like all the strongest trainers were being funneled into his bracket.

Dino and Remo were already tough, but now he had fought Gordie and Kabu—both legitimate Gym Leader-level trainers.

Kabu was even stronger than Gordie.

Because while Gordie had yet to officially become a Gym Leader, Kabu had only stepped down temporarily—and Johan knew he'd eventually reclaim his position at Motostoke Stadium.

Under normal circumstances, these guys were at least quarterfinal contenders, if not potential champions.

Cynthia frowned slightly and nodded.

"I'll ask around."

"Uhh, no need," Johan quickly backtracked with an awkward laugh.

He didn't actually think the Sinnoh League was rigging anything—major organizations didn't operate like that.

Still… it was a little suspicious.

Flint rubbed his chin thoughtfully and posed a hypothesis.

"Maybe…" he said, stroking his imaginary beard, "it's just Professor Johan's luck?"

Johan: "…"

He smiled and clapped Flint on the shoulder.

"Flint, Garchomp wants to spar with you."

"Nice! Let's do it!"

Flint was completely unfazed, even looking excited.

"Garrr!! (▼皿▼#)"

Nearby, Garchomp—its mouth covered in whipped cream—bared its fangs with a low growl.

Why the hell should I listen to you?!

Johan added:

"No restrictions."

Flint: "???"

"Garrr."

That evening—

Having promised Garchomp a feast, Johan was now hard at work.

If it was just one Garchomp, things would be manageable.

But the problem was—

"Ka-kaah!"

Finished!

Gengar held up its empty bowl.

"Yoji!"

Larvitar lifted its "bedpan."

Me too!

"Garrr—hic—"

Garchomp let out a burp, blinking in disbelief.

No way.

It could accept losing to Gengar in a eating contest—

But to a baby Larvitar?!

Unforgivable.

"Garrr!"

Give me another half bowl!

Johan rubbed his forehead.

This is how my savings disappear.

Pokéblocks were meant to be snacks, not full meals!

This was all Flint's fault.

He'd pay for this.

Somewhere, Flint sneezed.

"Huh? Did I catch a cold?"

Just then—

"Incoming call, Roto!"

Rotom hovered over with Johan's phone.

Johan, seizing the perfect escape, grabbed the phone and walked onto the balcony.

Without even checking the caller ID, he picked up.

"Johan, it's me."

A familiar voice came through.

Johan glanced at the screen.

"Professor Sycamore?"

"I'm organizing a research conference soon. Would you be interested in attending?"

"A research conference?"

"Yes—about Mega Evolution."

Johan immediately recalled something.

Previously, Professor Sycamore had mentioned his plan to publicly release part of his research on Mega Evolution.

He had even shown Johan a draft paper detailing his current findings.

Although many aspects of Mega Evolution were still not fully understood, Sycamore believed that such a groundbreaking discovery should be shared with the world as soon as possible.

"A research conference…" Johan mused.

"Yes. Since you're participating in the Lily of the Valley Conference, I've scheduled it for after the tournament ends," Sycamore said.

Clearly, he had been keeping an eye on the tournament—both he and his assistant Alain had been following the matches closely.

But then, as if struck by a realization, Sycamore's voice suddenly took on an excited tone.

"Wait a minute…"

Johan could feel the expectation in his voice.

"Are you saying… you'll be showcasing Mega Evolution during this tournament?"

That would be huge.

Given the massive audience watching the Lily of the Valley Conference, if Johan demonstrated Mega Evolution in an official battle, the impact would be astronomical.

Johan blinked.

"…Did I say that?"

But then again—

It wasn't entirely impossible.

"Let's just say the chances are low but not zero."

He couldn't say for sure.

After analyzing Tobias's battles, Johan felt that—even with Darkrai and Latios—his opponent hadn't shown anything yet that truly forced him to reveal Mega Evolution.

Unless—

Tobias was secretly hiding four more Legendary Pokémon.

Now that would be fun.

If Tobias really had an entire team of Mythicals and Legendaries…

Then Johan wouldn't hold back.

They'd go down together.

"That's…" Sycamore hesitated before laughing. "Well, let's see how things go. I'll be watching closely!"

Johan nodded.

"Oh, by the way," he added, "I heard that after the tournament, a noble from Sinnoh plans to host an exhibition match—probably to capitalize on the hype. They're inviting some top trainers, and they might even extend invitations to the Elite Four."

This was information Johan had learned from Cynthia.

If he won the Lily of the Valley Conference, there was a high chance he'd be invited.

And if that happened…

The timing would be perfect.

The tournament's attention would still be at its peak, but the exhibition match wouldn't have the same intense competitive pressure.

A public debut of Mega Evolution at an exhibition match would be ideal.

"Hmm…" Sycamore murmured in thought.

"If that's the case, maybe we should wait a little longer," he agreed. "That'll also give me more time to finalize some of my research."

"Sounds good," Johan said.

"Alright then—focus on your battles, and good luck in the tournament!"

"Don't worry, I'm sweeping through this."

# 415: As Long as I Knock Them All Out Instantly, Stamina Won’t Be an Issue!

January 21st.

The day of the Lily of the Valley Conference's quarterfinals had arrived.

"Today is!!"

"The battle for the semifinals!!"

The host's energetic voice echoed through the Lily of the Valley Conference stadium.

Tobias stood at the highest railing of the venue, leaning on it slightly as Darkrai emerged from behind him.

Noticing Darkrai, Tobias remained expressionless and casually asked,

"Do you think Mr. Johan will sweep all six today?"

"That depends on which Pokémon he sends out."

Recently, whenever Tobias had free time, he would come to watch Johan's battles in person. Darkrai, as a result, was "forced" to watch many as well, so it had developed a general understanding of Johan's strength.

"What do you mean?" Tobias grew interested.

"If it's that Gengar, then it shouldn't be a problem," Darkrai said seriously.

Tobias found something amusing.

He highly admired Johan, while Darkrai greatly admired Gengar.

Perhaps that was the bond between them.

"Then tell me, how confident are you if you face Gengar?" Tobias asked curiously.

Darkrai pondered for a moment before saying slowly, "Sixty-forty."

Tobias showed a hint of surprise and remarked,

"I remember Mr. Johan once said something very interesting."

"What was it?"

"There are three great illusions in life: 'My phone is ringing,' 'She likes me,' and 'I can counterattack successfully.'"

Darkrai's expression darkened for a moment, but it quickly turned into a teasing smirk.

"Who likes you?"

"Hey!" Tobias was furious. "Duel me!"

Darkrai sneered. "Bring it on!"

Saying that, it pulled out a deck of cards.

After a brief pause, it added, "Don't you dare use my card!"

Tobias smirked. "Not only will I use it, but I'll use the UR version!"

Meanwhile.

Johan and Kabu stepped onto the battlefield at the host's signal.

Cheers erupted in the circular arena.

Kabu, a middle-aged man in his late 30s or early 40s, wore a standard Galar region red trainer outfit, with the number "187" printed on it, symbolizing "Spark."

A towel was draped around his neck, and sweat glistened on his cheeks as he lightly panted, as if he had just finished exercising.

Once a Gym Leader but now stripped of his title, Kabu, however, showed no sign of discouragement.

As he put it:

"I'm still young. As long as I fight with the spirit of the young, it's all good! Strength speaks for itself—simple as that."

Not everyone is willing to abandon everything and start over.

Kabu was definitely a trainer worthy of respect.

"Mr. Johan, is it okay if I call you that?" Kabu wiped the remaining sweat off his forehead with his towel.

"Of course, Mr. Kabu. You can also just call me Johan," Johan replied with a smile.

Kabu nodded, his eyes gradually growing determined. "I've been looking forward to this battle for a long time, so please, show me your full strength!"

Johan's expression also became serious as he replied solemnly, "Of course!"

"Trainers, send out your Pokémon!" the referee signaled.

"Go, Torkoal!"

Without hesitation, Kabu tossed his first Poké Ball.

This match, which would determine a spot in the semifinals, followed a 6v6 rotational battle format.

"Oooh~!"

As Torkoal appeared, the burning coal on its back released a scorching orange beam into the sky.

Buzz—

In an instant.

The sunlight over the entire battlefield became more intense.

Drought ability activated!

The already clear weather turned into Harsh Sunlight!

Everyone knew Torkoal—nicknamed "Little Groudon"—was one of the top weather setters for sun teams. It could even excel in Trick Room strategies with a devastating Overheat.

And the Pokémon Johan sent out was—

"Ku~!!"

From the red light emerged a small, gray-and-white bear with an intensely serious expression. It tightened its headband and waved its fists, exuding an aura of determination.

This was none other than Johan's elite Kubfu!

"Who dares challenge me? State your name! I do not fight nameless opponents!"

"Oooh?"

Torkoal blinked in confusion and looked at Kabu.

What did it just say? Why does it sound like complete nonsense?

"Battle begin!"

"Torkoal, use Overheat!"

Kabu gave his command without hesitation.

"Oooh!!"

Torkoal wasn't fazed by Kubfu's words. It lifted its head and roared, as flames erupted from the holes in its shell.

The next moment, molten lava-like fire spewed from Torkoal's mouth, engulfing the battlefield.

Overheat's power starts at 150, and with the boost from Harsh Sunlight, if it hit Kubfu, even if it didn't faint immediately, the battle would be tough going forward.

But Johan had clearly anticipated this, so he remained calm and commanded:

"Kubfu, use Dig!"

"Ku~!"

In an instant, dirt and dust flew up as Kubfu vanished underground.

Kabu frowned slightly and said in a deep voice, "Torkoal, use Smokescreen!"

Whooosh—

Thick black smoke spewed from Torkoal's mouth, completely enveloping its body, making it difficult for Kubfu to locate it.

Buzz—

But what they didn't know was that Kubfu had already locked onto Torkoal using Aura!

To a Pokémon that could sense Aura, Smokescreen was practically useless.

Boom!!

The ground suddenly exploded, and a devastating underground strike flipped Torkoal over.

"Now, use Brick Break!"

"Ku!!"

Kubfu seized the moment, targeting Torkoal's weaker underbelly, and delivered a powerful punch.

BAM!!

With a pained cry, Torkoal was sent flying before crashing heavily to the ground.

"A critical hit! Torkoal is unable to battle!" The referee waved his flag, declaring the result.

The Battle Continued

"That was fast!"

"A critical hit! Otherwise, Torkoal's defense wouldn't have fallen that quickly."

"This battle style is totally different from Johan's previous matches!"

Despite Torkoal's loss, Kabu showed little change in expression.

His Drought-Torkoal strategy was designed to go all-in with one powerful move. Since Overheat missed, its low Speed meant it wouldn't be able to mount another offense effectively.

But it had done its job.

The harsh sunlight still burned intensely.

And through that exchange, Kabu had also gained a newfound respect for Kubfu's Dig strategy.

Taking a deep breath, he quickly tossed out his second Poké Ball.

"Kabu's second Pokémon is... Scovillain!"

It had a humanoid form, with green skin, and two heads resembling chili peppers—one green and one red.

Scovillain, the Spicy Pokémon, was a Grass/Fire-type said to have originated from Paldea. It was an excellent Sun Team mixed wall and speed attacker.

Under the Harsh Sunlight, Scovillain's body shimmered with light.

Chlorophyll ability activated!

[Chlorophyll: Doubles Speed during Harsh Sunlight.]

The battle resumed.

"Scovillain, use Solar Beam!"

Bright light rapidly gathered around Scovillain's two ferocious heads.

Normally, Solar Beam required charging, but under Harsh Sunlight, it fired instantly.

However, Johan gave a command that stunned everyone:

"Charge forward, Kubfu!"

Kubfu didn't hesitate for even a second—or rather, its thoughts were completely in sync with Johan's. It took a deep breath, sharpened its focus, and charged straight at Scovillain.

The opponent was fast, but if it could close the distance, the advantage would be back in its hands!

No Dig this time.

Because while Dig was sometimes a great move, if the opponent predicted the narrow tunnel's location, Kubfu could become an easy target.

Buzz—

Scovillain's Solar Beam blasted forward, radiating intense heat and light, aimed directly at Kubfu.

"Break through it!" Johan commanded, narrowing his eyes.

Kubfu, with its full concentration, swung its fists straight into the beam.

Boom!!

Light scattered in all directions, as if fire itself had been shattered by sheer force.

Kubfu had seen through it!

To the spectators, it looked as though Kubfu was punching through Solar Beam with its bare hands, evoking the image of someone deflecting a Kamehameha with pure strength.

The distance between the two Pokémon closed rapidly, in a way no one expected.

Kubfu exuded overwhelming momentum.

And when it came to momentum—Kabu prided himself on never backing down!

If Johan was going for an all-out offensive approach today, then Kabu would respond in kind!

"Scovillain, use Overheat!"

Whoosh—

The two pepper-shaped heads of Scovillain began to move. The green head released a burst of spicy green powder, while the red head prepared to unleash all of its stored firepower.

Johan's lips curled into a slight grin, his gaze sharp as he clenched his fist.

"Close combat!"

Most of the audience didn't even see Kubfu's movement—it was just a blur of white light, followed by an explosion in the ground.

In an instant, Kubfu appeared directly in front of Scovillain.

Then, it spread its arms wide—before unleashing a barrage of punches as fast as falling raindrops, each one hitting harder than the last.

Such overwhelming power!

Speed and strength were often interchangeable—but sometimes, pure strength could also create speed!

Still, Scovillain's flames erupted at the same time.

The two attacks clashed in the most direct way possible!

But—there was still technique involved.

For example, Kubfu deliberately targeted Scovillain's red chili head, striking it several times in quick succession, forcing it backward—thus redirecting its flames away from itself.

And that…

Decided the battle.

BOOM—!!

Scovillain was sent flying, both of its four eyes spiraling into swirls.

Kubfu, still wreathed in lingering flames, shook them off with a powerful stance, its fur scorched black, but its eyes shining with fierce determination.

"Ku!! (▼〇▼*)"

It spread its arms wide and let out a victorious cry.

"I don't know why… but this battle just fired me up!"

"Johan's tactical skills are impressive, but… I think I like this straightforward brawler style even more!"

"But Kubfu's condition isn't looking great either. Close Combat is a high-risk move—it leaves the user worn down afterward."

"At this rate, there's no way it can sweep all six, right?"

Everyone knew that reckless brawler strategies, unless backed by a huge level advantage, usually maxed out at a 3-0 streak before running out of stamina.

But…

Condition?

Kubfu simply clenched its fists and took a battle stance again.

It was in peak condition!

Because as long as it could knock out every opponent in one hit—

Then stamina wasn't an issue at all!

# 416: Who Isn’t a Legendary Pokémon at This Point!!

Losing two Pokémon put some pressure on Kabu.

He hadn't expected that in a direct exchange, Scovillain would ultimately fail to take down Kubfu.

However, just as the audience had noted, Kubfu's condition wasn't great.

On top of that, after using Close Combat, its defenses were lowered—this was the perfect time to finish it off!

This time, Kabu was a little more cautious.

Finally, he threw out his third Poké Ball, summoning his third Pokémon.

"Kabu's third Pokémon—Arcanine!"

A large canine Pokémon with a thick, flaxen-colored mane, a body covered in tiger-like black and orange stripes, and an expression that exuded majesty.

Arcanine, the Legendary Pokémon, Fire-type!

Arcanine was a Pokémon of ancient Eastern legends, once said to have fought alongside a great warrior to govern a nation.

Back in the day, Arcanine truly existed as a Legendary Pokémon.

"Oowoo—!!"

A long howl echoed, carrying an intimidating presence.

Intimidate Ability, activated!

Kubfu tensed up, its fur bristling under the overwhelming pressure of Arcanine's roar, but it gritted its teeth and refused to take a step back.

"Ku!!"

In fact, Kubfu roared back at Arcanine.

A faint aura of Aura energy surged around Kubfu, rippling through its body.

Hmm?

Noticing this, even Johan was slightly surprised.

Arcanine's presence had actually further awakened Kubfu's Aura?

Though Kubfu's physical condition seemed poor, Johan could sense that its mental state was exceptionally strong—stronger than ever before!

"Arcanine, Extreme Speed!"

Kabu barked his command.

Whether or not Arcanine's Intimidate had worked, its role was clear—to finish off Kubfu as quickly as possible.

A white glow enveloped Arcanine as it lunged at Kubfu with the wind at its back.

Aura!

Johan's eyes gleamed faintly, syncing with Kubfu's Aura.

To them, Arcanine's movements seemed significantly slower.

"Detect! Block it!"

BAM!!

From the audience's perspective, all they saw was Arcanine turning into a streak of white light—then came a muffled impact as Arcanine's front paws clashed with Kubfu's hands.

More precisely, Kubfu had caught Arcanine's front paws.

Kabu and Johan's expressions turned serious at the same time.

"Arcanine, Flare Blitz!"

Shhh!!

Intense flames erupted from Arcanine's body, engulfing it instantly. Given their close proximity, Kubfu couldn't avoid being affected.

"Focus Punch!"

Kabu and Arcanine sought to use their close range to deal damage—but Johan had the same idea.

Kubfu let out a low growl, ignoring the flames licking at its body. One paw held Arcanine's leg tightly, while the other clenched into a fist and drew back.

A dazzling white glow enveloped its fist.

Power surged within, gathering to a peak before Kubfu hurled its punch forward!

"Ku!!"

Kubfu's Focus Punch crashed down like a tidal wave, drawing gasps from the audience.

"Yes!!" Flint clenched his fist tightly, as if he were the one battling instead of Kubfu. "Holding the opponent in place so it can't dodge—this Focus Punch is guaranteed to hit!"

Lucian and the others, despite their excitement, couldn't help but feel a bit worried.

Flint's strategy was exhilarating, but… Kubfu was likely at its limit.

BAM!!

The punch struck Arcanine square on the head. With a whimper, it was sent flying, its flames extinguished in an instant.

Arcanine flinched, its head dazed from the impact.

Kubfu staggered slightly but didn't hesitate to chase after it.

Johan furrowed his brows slightly but still commanded firmly:

"Close Combat!"

A flurry of relentless attacks rained down on Arcanine, giving it no chance to dodge or counter.

By the time Kubfu's assault ceased, Arcanine had already collapsed, unable to battle.

Kubfu, breathing heavily, plopped down onto the ground.

"Arcanine is unable to battle!"

Woah—

"That was a comeback kill?!"

"Other people's comebacks are flukes, but when it's Johan, it's real?"

"Still, Kubfu looks completely exhausted."

"There's no way it can survive this too, right?"

Kubfu truly was at its limit.

Unlike Charcadet, which could sustain itself with its Life Fire and practically acted like it had a Leftovers item, Kubfu had no means of recovery.

And Kabu was no joke—he was a Gym Leader, after all. His Pokémon posed a serious threat to Kubfu.

Kabu recalled Arcanine and wiped his forehead with a towel.

The pressure was intense.

He hadn't expected Kubfu to survive that exchange.

Taking a deep breath, Kabu calmed himself. He realized he had been too impatient, too eager to finish off Kubfu, which had disrupted his usual battle rhythm.

With this realization, Kabu quickly adjusted his mindset.

Regardless of Kubfu's current state, he would treat it as if it were still at full strength.

With that in mind…

Kabu pulled out his fourth Poké Ball and tossed it forward.

A Pokémon with a crimson body, burning flames along its tendril-like appendages, and a menacing presence appeared—Centiskorch!

Centiskorch, the Radiator Pokémon, Fire- and Bug-type, and Kabu's signature Pokémon!

Upon entering the battlefield, its flame-like tendrils flared up intensely.

"Centiskorch, Will-O-Wisp!" Kabu commanded.

Since Kubfu was a physical attacker, burning it to lower its offensive power was the safest and most effective strategy!

"Ssshh—"

Centiskorch hissed, its heater-like abdomen glowing as it launched a ball of eerie blue flames toward Kubfu.

At the same time, Johan pulled out a Poké Ball.

He knew Kubfu's condition better than anyone—there was no need to force it to keep fighting just to satisfy the crowd's desire for a one-on-six performance.

Kubfu had already done more than enough. He was proud of it.

However…

Kubfu braced itself against its knees and stood up, shaking its head firmly at Johan.

Huh?

Kubfu grinned, revealing a goofy smile.

"Ku~!"

What kind of general runs from battle?!

If I fight, I fight until the last moment! If I fall, I fall on the battlefield!

Johan: ...

Yeah… He should probably stop telling Kubfu about Romance of the Three Kingdoms. Maybe switch to Makka Pakka stories instead.

But Kubfu's eyes were resolute.

Panting heavily, its thick eyebrows and sharp eyes burned with the desire for battle.

Why should Tobias and Darkrai be the only ones allowed to sweep teams? Am I not just as good as them?!

After all my training, after all the "blessings" I've received from nature—

Who isn't a Legendary Pokémon at this point!!

"Ku!!"

Ignoring its injuries, Kubfu twisted its body and delivered a powerful kick.

BAM!!

The Will-O-Wisp was shattered on impact, its embers scattering around Kubfu like falling stars.

Standing tall amidst the flickering flames, Kubfu made its stance clear.

"This Kubfu…"

Kabu muttered, his gaze sharpening. Even he could feel Kubfu's unyielding determination.

It reminded him of himself.

Once, he had been a renowned Gym Leader, respected by many—only to lose it all due to his own arrogance. But he never gave up. He persevered and climbed back up.

No wonder Kubfu had fought this far.

"You are a worthy opponent. So—Centiskorch, Inferno Overdrive!!"

Centiskorch's fiery tendrils blazed, its heater glowing red-hot as a tidal wave of flames surged forward.

Then—

Facing the roaring inferno—

Johan exhaled softly, a relaxed smile forming on his lips.

Kubfu, staring straight into the flames, wore the exact same smile.

The fire swallowed Kubfu whole.

But if anyone with Aura vision were present, they would see it—Johan's and Kubfu's Auras intertwining.

Johan's Aura poured into Kubfu like a raging river.

"Then let's fight—

Urshifu!!"

BOOM!!

From within the inferno, an explosion of energy erupted—followed by a radiant white glow.

The crowd erupted.

"Wait… is it—?!"

"Holy crap, it's evolving!"

"Kubfu evolved at the most critical moment?!"

A battlefield evolution!

As the brilliant light enveloped Kubfu, its small frame expanded rapidly.

The years of relentless training, the honed muscles—now fully realized.

Standing over two meters tall, its chiseled physique exuded raw power.

Its gray fur rippled in the wind, its headband fluttering.

The embodiment of strength and elegance—Urshifu had arrived.

"Garruu!!"

With a mighty roar, Urshifu's aura skyrocketed, dispersing the surrounding flames.

"Holy crap, it really evolved!"

"I see it now! Joha

n used evolution to restore Kubfu to full strength!"

"Wait… that's allowed?!"

"Who said Kubfu couldn't recover? Evolution solves everything!"

"…"

Staring at the now towering and battle-ready Urshifu, the audience fell into stunned silence.

"Garruu!!"

Urshifu has entered the battlefield!!

To continue where we left off:

After the previous battle, I contacted another victim through Officer Jenny and the former owner of the breeding house.

I figured that since we were both victims, we could discuss how to take down that damn Pokémon hunter together.

So, we decided to meet up for a meal first and then talk things through.

And guess what?

I had no idea before, but when I saw them in person, I was shocked.

The other victim turned out to be a stunning, fair-skinned, wealthy beauty! And if there was one thing that stood out about her—it was this:

Rich, rich, and rich!

She told me that while she was upset about getting scammed, she had no real way to recover her losses, so she just wrote it off—after all, it was just a small sum to her.

I immediately thought, Man, if only I could have that kind of "small sum" too... Ahem.

I told her that we couldn't just let criminals roam free, so…
